Refers to Mercury, the quickening, communicative, and transformative principle in alchemical thought, which Böhme links to the Holy Spirit.</note></term>. And an angel must also make use of this, otherwise he cannot be a moving creature; for he must also eat of the heavenly fruits through the mouth.

You must not understand this in an earthly way, however; for an angel has no intestines, nor any flesh or bone; rather, he is joined together by the divine power in a form and manner like a human, also with all limbs like a human; but he has neither the <insert>reproductive organs</insert> <note>original: *Geburtsglieder*.</note> nor any exit from below, nor does he need them. For man received his reproductive organs, and also his exit, only in the lamentable Fall. An angel expels nothing from himself except the divine power, which he grasps with the mouth, with which he kindles his heart, and the heart kindles all the limbs; he expels the same

# Morning Redness in its Rising.
H 2

...with their hands and eat them, just as we humans do; but they have no need of teeth for this, nor do they have any, for the fruit consists of divine power.

Now, all of this—whatever an angel uses that is outside of himself for the sustenance of his life—is not his bodily property which he holds by <term>natural right <note>original: *Natur-Recht*. In Böhme's thought, this refers to the inherent laws governing a being's existence based on its created nature.</note></term>. Rather, the heavenly Father gives it all to them out of love. Their <note>original: *corpus*. Latin for "body."</note> body, indeed, is their own property, for God has given it to them as a possession. And whatever is given to someone as their own or as property belongs to them by natural right; and anyone who takes it back from them without cause <note>original: *ohne gedinge*. Literally "without condition" or "without legal grounds."</note> does not act rightly. God also does not do this; therefore, an angel is an eternal, imperishable creature that exists for all eternity.

But now, what use would the body be to him if God did not feed it? He would have no mobility and would lie there like dead wood. For this reason, the angels are obedient to God and humble themselves before the mighty God; they laud, honor, boast of, and praise Him in His great wondrous deeds, and they constantly sing of God’s holiness because He feeds them.

True love in the divine nature originates from the <term>wellspring <note>original: *Brunnquell*. A favorite image of Böhme’s, depicting the divine persons as flowing fountains of energy.</note></term> of the Son of God. Behold, you child of man, let this be told to you: the angels already know well what true love toward God is; you must [receive] the same into your cold heart—

# Morning Redness in its Rising.

Mark this: when the lovely, joyful radiance and light, with the sweet power from the Son of God, shines within the whole Father into all powers, then all powers are ignited with the lovely light and sweet power, becoming triumphant and joyful.

So it is also when the lovely and joyful light of the Son of God shines upon the beloved angels and shimmers into their hearts; then all the powers in their bodies are ignited, and such a joyful fire of love arises that they laud, sing, and ring out in great joy—which neither I, nor any creature, can express in words.

With this song, I wish to have summoned <note>original: *citiret*. Böhme uses a legal term meaning to "summon" or "cite" a person to appear, suggesting the reader is being formally called to witness the afterlife.</note> the reader into that life; there you will experience it yourself, for I cannot write it down.

But if you wish to experience it in this life, then cease from your hypocrisy, <term>scheming <note>original: *Finanzen*. In 17th-century German, this term referred specifically to dishonest financial maneuvers, trickery, or self-interested political intrigue.</note></term>, and deceit, as well as from your mocking. Turn your heart with total earnestness toward God, and perform penance for your sins with a truly earnest intention to live holily. Pray to God for His Holy Spirit and wrestle with Him, just as the holy patriarch <term>Jacob</term> wrestled with Him all night until the <term>Morning Redness</term> <note>original: *Morgenröhte*. This is the titular "Aurora," symbolizing the moment of spiritual breakthrough and the dawning of divine light in the soul.</note> broke, and did not let go until He had blessed him (Genesis 32). You must do the same, and the Holy Spirit will surely take shape within you.

If you do not let up in your earnestness, then this fire will suddenly come upon you and gaze at you; then you will surely experience what I have written here and will truly believe my book. You will also become quite another

<summary>Böhme describes how the light of the Son of God ignites the "fire of love" within the Father and the angels, leading to unspeakable joy. He challenges the reader to move beyond intellectual reading and experience this light themselves by wrestling with God in earnest prayer, similar to the biblical Jacob.</summary>

# The Day-spring in its Rising. 120

...richly / like an angelic wisdom / but not within my own reason or comprehension or perfection as if I were an angel / but piecemeal / only for as long as the Spirit remains in me; beyond that I cannot recognize it. When the Spirit departs from me, I know nothing but the elemental and earthly things of this world; but the Spirit looks even into the depth of the <term>Godhead</term> <note>original: "Gottheit"</note>.

Now one might ask: How is it a matter of <term>substance</term> <note>original: "substantz" — here referring to the essential reality or spiritual-physical makeup of the heavens</note> that the Son of God is born in the midst of these kingdoms? For then surely one <term>angelic host</term> <note>original: "Englisch Heer"</note> would be closer to Him than another, since their realm is of such great depth? Also, outside of these kingdoms, would the radiance and power of the Son of God not be as great as it is for those who are near Him, and as it is within the angelic territories?

### Answer.

The <term>holy angels</term> <note>original: "H. Engel"</note> were made into creatures out of God for this reason: so that they should praise, sing, ring out, rejoice, and increase the heavenly joy before God’s Heart—which is the Son of God. And where else would the Father appoint them but before the door of His Heart? For just as all human joy, which resides in the whole person, arises from the <term>wellspring</term> <note>original: "Brunnquell"</note> of the heart, so also in God the great joy arises from the wellspring of His Heart.

Therefore He created the holy angels out of Himself; they are like little gods according to the essence and qualities of the whole God, so that they might play, praise, and sing within the divine power—

# The Day-spring in its Rising. 121

...and ring out, and increase the rising joy from the **Heart of God**.

The radiance, however, and the power of the **Son of God**—or the **Heart of God**, which is the light or **wellspring** <note>original: "Quell"</note> of joys—takes its most beautiful and most joyful origin in the midst of these kingdoms, and shines in and through all the <term>Angelic Portals</term> <note>original: "Englische Porten" — Böhme uses this term to describe the various spheres or spiritual "gateways" that constitute the heavenly realm.</note>.

But you must understand exactly how this is intended; for when I speak in a <term>parable</term> <note>original: "Gleichnis"</note> and compare the Son of God to the sun or a round sphere, it is not meant to imply that He is a measurable wellspring that one could measure, or whose depth, beginning, or end one could fathom. I only write in such metaphors so that the reader might arrive at the correct understanding. For it is not the meaning that the Son of God should be born *only* in the midst of these Angelic Portals and not also outside of the Angelic Portals. After all, the Father’s powers are everywhere—from which and out of which the Son is born, and from which the **Holy Spirit** proceeds. How then could He be born only in the midst of these Angelic Portals?

This alone is the basis and the meaning: that the holy **Father**, who is All, has His most joyful and most loving qualities within these Angelic Portals; out of these, the most joyful and most loving Light, Word, Heart of power, or <term>wellspring</term> <note>original: "Brunnquell"</note> is born. Therefore, He also created the holy angels in these places for His joy, honor, and <term>glory</term> <note>original: "Herzlichkeit" — while this modernly means "cordiality," Böhme uses it here to denote the magnificent, heart-centered splendor of the divine presence.</note>.

# The Day-spring in its Rising. 123

...instead of climbing until he might reach the high mysteries. It is not the meaning, however, that the Son of God is a shaped image <note>original: "figuriret Bildniß" — a structured or finite form</note> similar to the sun; for if that were so, then the Son of God would have to have a beginning, and the Father would have to have born him at a single point in time. In that case, He would not be an eternal, almighty Son of the Father, but would be like a king who had a greater king over him, who had fathered him in time and who had the power to change him.

That would be a son who had a beginning, and his power and luster would be like the power of the sun, which goes out from the sun while the **body** <note>original: "corpus"</note> or sphere of the sun remains in its place. Now, if this were so, then one **Angelic Portal** <note>original: "Englische Porte" — Böhme's term for the various regions or dimensions of the heavenly realm</note> would certainly be much closer to the Son of God than another. But I will show you here the highest portal of the divine mystery, and you need seek no higher, for there is also none higher.

Observe: the Father's power is everything in and above all heavens, and that same power begets light everywhere. Now, that **All-Power** <note>original: "Alle-Krafft"</note> is and is called the Father; and the light that is born out of that same All-Power is and is called the Son.

It is called the Son, however, because it is born out of the Father, so that it is the Father's Heart within his powers. And once it is born, it is a different person than the Father; for the Father is the power and the kingdom, and the Son

# The Day-spring in its Rising. 115

Gold-stone <note>original: "Goldstein" — refers to gold-bearing ore or the "stone" in which gold is found</note> / which is undivided; first there is the *materia* <note>original: "materia" — Latin for matter or substance</note>, that is the saltpeter <note>original: "Salniter" — in Böhme’s thought, saltpeter represents a foundational, explosive divine power or "nitre"</note> and Mercury <note>original: "Marcurius" — the "driving" or "mobile" quality in alchemy</note>, which is the Mother or the whole stone / which gives birth everywhere in the whole stone to the gold / and in the gold is the hearty power of the stone.

Now / the saltpeter and Mercury signify the Father / the gold signifies the Son / the power signifies the Holy Spirit: in such a way is also the Trinity in the Holy Trinity / except that within it everything moves and goes out.

One also finds in a gold-stone perhaps in one place a point where more and more beautiful gold is inside / than in another / regardless of the fact that gold is in the whole stone: so also is the *locus* <note>original: "locus" — Latin for "place" or "location"</note> or place in the midst of the **Angelic Portals** <term>Angelic Portals: Böhme’s term for the various regions, dimensions, or "gates" of the heavenly realm</term> a dear / beautiful / and gracious place to the Father / where his Son and Heart is born in the most loving way / and where the Holy Spirit goes out from the Father and Son in the most loving way.

Thus you have the true ground of this mystery / and you must not think / that the Son of God was born out of the Father all at once / at a certain time / as if he had a beginning / and now stands there as a King / and lets himself be worshipped.

No / that would not be an eternal Son / but rather he would have a beginning / and would be beneath the Father / who had begotten him: He could also not be all-knowing / for he would not know how it had been / before the Father had begotten him: instead the Son is always born from eternity to eternity / and shines always from eternity to eternity

## Concerning the Foundation and the Mystery.

If one wishes to discover the mystery / and the deepest foundation / then one must look upon and contemplate with diligence the creation of this world / its governance and order / as well as the qualities of the stars and elements. Although this is a corrupt and twofold being / and is also not living or understanding in itself / for it is only the corrupt Salniter <term>Salniter: A term Böhme uses to describe the "salt-niter" or the fundamental divine matter/energy from which all physical and spiritual qualities are formed</term> and Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius" — the alchemical principle of motion, vitality, and the "word" of nature</note> in which King Lucifer held house / and in which there is both evil and good / although it is nonetheless the true power of God / which was bright and pure before its corruption / just as it is now in heaven.

These powers of the stars and elements were, by the Creator / after the horrible fall of Lucifer’s kingdom / set back into such an order / as the kingdom of the angels stood in divine splendor <note>original: "Pomp" — here meaning magnificence or glorious display</note> before his fall. Only, you should not think / that the Angelic Kingdom with its creatures was ever rotated or turned about / as the stars are now / which are merely powers / and because of the birth of this world are thus turned around.

The birth of which stands in the welling anguish <note>original: "quellenden Angst" — Böhme uses "Angst" to describe the intense contraction and pressure of spiritual birth, like birth pangs or a tightening force</note> between evil and good / in corruption and redemption / until the end of this enumeration <note>original: "enumeration" — likely referring to the calculated time until the end of the world</note> or Judgment Day.

## The 8th Chapter.

### Concerning the Great Mystery of the Entire Body <note>original: "Corpus"; Böhme uses the Latin term to emphasize the collective, organic unity of the kingdom.</note> of an Angelic Kingdom.

The angelic kingdoms are formed throughout according to the divine Being <note>original: "Wesen"; this refers to the fundamental essence or substance of God.</note>, and they have no other shape than that which the divine Being has in its Trinity. The only difference is this: that their bodies are creatures which have a beginning and an end <note>In Böhme's thought, angels are "created" out of eternal nature, meaning they have a point of origin even if they are immortal.</note>, and the kingdom wherein they have their territory <note>original: "Revier"; a district, province, or domain.</note> is not their physical property held by natural right <note>original: "Natur-recht"</note>—in the same way they possess their bodies by natural right—but rather the kingdom belongs to God the Father. He created them out of His powers and may place them wherever He wills; otherwise, their body is made according to and out of all the powers of the Father.

And their power brings forth the light and knowledge <note>original: "Erkäntnis"; spiritual perception or intuitive understanding.</note> within them, just as God brings forth His Son out of all His powers. And just as the Holy Spirit proceeds from all the powers of the Father and the Son, so also in an angel, their spirit proceeds from their heart, from their light, and from all their powers.

### Here note the Depth.

In God the Father is all power <term>Krafft: Böhme's specific term for the active, creative energies or "qualities" of the divine being.</term>, and He is the wellspring of all powers in His depth. In Him is light and darkness, air and water, heat and cold, hard and soft, thick and thin, sound and tone, sweet and sour, bitter and sharp, and more than I can recount. I recognize this solely by my own body, for it was originally made from Adam out of all these powers and according to the image of God.

But you must not think here that the powers in God the Father exist in such a way, or interact <term>qualificiren: Böhme uses this to describe how various qualities "flavor," bond, or operate with one another to produce a specific state of being.</term> in such a corrupted manner as they do in a human being—which Lord Lucifer has caused to be so—but rather, everything is fine, lovely, and full of delight, and entirely gentle. 

First, the light is (if I may compare it to something in nature) like the light of the sun, but not so unbearable as the light of the sun is to our corrupted eyes; instead, it is entirely lovely and blissful, a vision of love. ### Of the second circumstance or <term>species: Latin for "kinds," "types," or "appearances." In Böhme's philosophy, these are the distinct modes through which the divine essence manifests.</term>

<meta>This page continues the exposition of the seven fountain-spirits from page 137. The previous page defined the first quality (harshness/cold) and the second quality (sweetness/warmth). Here, Böhme introduces the third quality, "bitterness," which provides the motion and friction necessary for life and joy.</meta>

...in your own mind you will be a harsh, blind stump.

### Concerning the third condition or species. <note>original: "species." Böhme uses this Latin term to mean a specific type, appearance, or manifestation of the divine essence.</note>

The third quality, or the third Spirit of God in the Father’s power, is the **bitter quality**; this is a penetrating or a compelling of the sweet and harsh qualities; it is trembling, penetrating, and rising up.

->Note here:<-

The harsh quality is the core or stock, or the sourness or contraction; and the sweet is the light alleviation or softening; and the bitter is the penetration or triumphing, which rises up and triumphs within the harsh and the sweet. This is the **fountain of joy** or the cause of laughing, uplifting joys, because of which a thing trembles and exults for joy; from this, heavenly joy originates. Furthermore, it is the formation of all kinds of red colors in its own quality; in the sweet [quality] it forms all kinds of white and blue; in the harsh and sour [quality] all kinds of green and dark and mixed colors, with various shapes and scents.

The bitter quality is the first Spirit from which life becomes active, from which mobility takes its origin, and is rightly called **HEART** <note>original: "COR" (Latin) and "HERTZ" (German). While Böhme previously associated the "Heart" with the harsh quality, he here identifies the bitter quality as the "Heart" because it acts as the pulse or engine of movement.</note>; for it is the trembling, conducting, elevating, penetrating Spirit, the triumphing or joy, an elevating fountain of laughter; in the sweet quality, the bitter is softened so that it becomes entirely lovely and joyful. But if it elevates itself too much,

Flesh does not burn or shine / only the fat burns and shines.

Now you might ask / how does this happen / or what is its nature? See / in the flesh, the harsh / sour and bitter qualities are <term>primary</term> <note>original: "Primus". Böhme uses this term to denote the dominant or ruling force in a spiritual or material composition.</note>, and in the fat resides the sweetness / therefore a fat creature is always more cheerful than a lean one / because the sweet spirit wells up more in it than in the lean one. For the light of nature / which is the spirit of life / shines more in it than in the lean / for in that same light within the sweet quality resides the triumphing or the joy / because the harsh and bitter qualities triumph within it / for they rejoice / that they are refreshed, fed, watered, and enlightened by the sweet and light quality. For in the harshness there is no life / but rather the harsh, cold, hard death / and in the bitterness there is no light / but the dark, bitter, and raging pain / the house of trembling and grim, fearful misery.

Therefore / when they are "guests" of the sweet and light quality / they become <term>permeated</term> <note>original: "inficiret". In Böhme's alchemical vocabulary, this refers to a process where one quality "infects" or transforms another into its own likeness.</note> and lovely / as well as entirely joyful / and they triumph within a creature: For this reason, no lean creature is cheerful / unless the heat within it is <term>primary</term> <note>original: "Primus"</note> / that is / even if it is lean / and has little fat / it still possesses an excellent sweetness. On the other hand, many a creature has much fat / and is yet entirely <term>melancholic</term> <note>Böhme links the four temperaments to the qualities of nature; melancholy here refers to a dominance of the heavy, dark, "elemental water" aspect despite the presence of physical fat.</note> / the reason is / that its fatness is inclined toward the elemental water / where the harsh and bitter qualities are somewhat strong.

If you are now a rational human / then see:

...rattling. Now, however, the word **BARM** <note>The first syllable of the German *Barmherzigkeit* (mercy). Böhme treats the sound of the word as a map of the spiritual process.</note> is a dead, unintelligible word that no one understands; it signifies that the two qualities—harsh and bitter—are a hard, dark, cold, and bitter essence which has no light in them. For that reason, one cannot understand their power outside of the light.

But when one says **BARM-HERTZ** <note>Literally "Mercy-Heart."</note>, one presses the second syllable, **HERTZ** [heart], out from the depths of the body, out from the heart. For the true spirit speaks the word **HERTZ** as it raises itself out of the heat of the heart, in which the light rises and wells up.

Now see: when you say **BARM**, the two qualities—harsh and bitter—form the word **BARM** very slowly together, for it is a long, faint syllable because of the weakness of those qualities. But when you say **HERTZ**, the spirit in the word **HERTZ** shoots out quickly like a flash of lightning, providing the word’s distinction and understanding. But when you say **IG** <note>The suffix *-ig* in German functions like "-ly" or "-ful," completing the word "Barmherzig" (Merciful).</note>, you catch the spirit in the middle of the other two qualities, so that it must remain within and form the word.

Such is the divine power; the harsh and bitter qualities are the <term>Salitter</term> <note>Böhme’s term for "divine saltpeter," referring to the fundamental, energetic "sap" or substance of the spiritual world.</note> of the divine omnipotence. The sweet quality is the core of Mercy, according to which the whole being with all powers is called GOD. The heat is the core of the spirit, from which the light proceeds and ignites itself in the midst of the sweet quality; it is then captured by the harsh and bitter qualities as if in their midst. Within this, the SON of GOD is born, and that is the true heart of GOD. And

### Understand this high thing rightly.

When the Father speaks the Word—that is, begets His Son, which occurs always and eternally—the same Word first takes its origin in the harsh quality <note>The first of Böhme's seven qualities, representing contraction and astringency.</note>; there it takes hold of itself; and in the sweet quality it takes its wellspring; and in the bitter quality it sharpens and moves itself; and in the heat it rises up and ignites the middle sweet wellspring.

Now it burns simultaneously in all qualities from the ignited fire, and the fire burns out of the qualities, for all qualities burn; and the same fire is one fire, and not many fires.

And the same fire is the true Son of God, who is born thus from eternity to eternity. I would wish to prove this by heaven and earth, stars and elements, and by all creatures—by stones, by leaves and grass, yes, by the devil himself—and not with dead, poor, unintelligible arguments <note>original: "argumenten"</note>, but with purely living and invincible, firm arguments, even above and against the reason <note>original: "vernunfft"</note> of all men, and finally against the gates of all devils and hell, if it would not take up too much and too wide a space here.

...occurred. Now, when he exalts himself, he rises up first in the **astringent quality** <term>original: "herbe qualität". Because of this, the astringent quality becomes so hard and sharp that it can no longer master the **sweet water** <note>The "sweet water" represents the gentle, fluid element of light, love, and divine life that should normally soften the harsher qualities.</note>, and it can no longer rise gently within the creature. Instead, it is captured by the astringent quality and dried up, and transformed into a hard, sharp, and fierce coldness. This represents the force of movement, agitation, and the "stinging" of life.</term> then rises within the dried-out sweet quality, it cannot soften the sweet one or soak it with its sweet, bright water because it is dried up. Latin for "body" or the physical/spiritual vessel of a being.</term> like a parched poison.

## Chapter 9.

### Concerning the Gracious, Friendly, and Merciful Love of GOD / The Great Heavenly and Divine Mystery.

Since I am writing here of heavenly and divine things, which are quite alien to the corrupt nature of man, the reader might without doubt wonder and be offended at the simplicity <note>original: "Einfalt". Böhme often uses this term to describe a lack of formal academic training, which he presents as a virtue that allows for direct divine inspiration.</note> of the author, because the impulse of corrupt nature looks only upon that which is high, like a proud, wild, lustful, and whorish woman, who in her heat always looks around for handsome men to dally with them.

So also is the haughty, corrupt nature of man; it looks only at what glitters and parades before the world, and supposes that God has forgotten the miserable man, and therefore plagues him so: She thinks the Holy Spirit looks only upon what is high, upon the art <term>art: "Kunst" in 17th-century German refers to formal scholarship, academic skill, or human ingenuity.</term> of this world, upon great and deep study.

But whether it truly be so, just look back, and you will find the foundation. Who was Abel? A shepherd. Who were Enoch and Noah? Simple people. Who were Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob? They were herdsmen of cattle. Who was Moses, the pre-

# Aurora: The Day-Spring.

->**The Dawning of the Day.**<-

**O** you poor human, turn back! The heavenly Father has stretched out both arms and calls to you: just come! He wants to embrace you in His love, for you are indeed His child; He loves you. With it you build your palace-houses, but I will seat you upon the seat of pestilence, and my Shepherd shall pasture my sheep forever.

## The 9th Chapter.

->Of the gracious, friendly, and merciful love of **GOD** /<-
->The great heavenly and Divine Mystery.<-

Since I am writing here of heavenly and divine things, which is quite foreign to the corrupted nature <term>corrupted nature: In Böhme’s theology, this refers to the state of humanity after the Fall, where our natural senses are "blinded" to the spiritual world and focused only on material gain.</term> of man, the reader will no doubt wonder and take offense at the simplicity <note>Böhme frequently reminds his readers that he is a simple cobbler without formal university training, using his "lowly" status to argue that his insights come from divine revelation rather than academic study.</note> of the author; for the drive of corrupted nature looks only upon that which is high, like a proud, wild, lustful, and whorish woman, who in her heat always looks around for handsome men to dally with.

So also is the haughty, corrupted nature of man; it looks only upon that which glitters and shines before the world, and supposes that God has forgotten the miserable man, and that for this reason He plagues him so. It thinks the Holy Spirit looks only upon high things, upon the art of this world, or upon great and deep study.

But as to whether it truly is so, just look back and you will find the foundation. Who was Abel? A shepherd. Who were Enoch and Noah? Simple people. Who were Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob? Now, how did our King Jesus Christ come into this world? poor and in great sorrow and misery, and had nowhere to lay his head, Matthew 8:20.

Who were his apostles? poor, despised, unlearned fishermen’s helpers. Who believed their sermons? the poor, lowly little people. The high priests and scribes <term>scribes: biblical scholars and legal experts, whom Böhme frequently compares to the university-trained theologians of his own time who opposed his "unlearned" writings.</term> were Christ’s executioners, who cried out: "Crucify him, crucify him!" <note>original: "Crucifige, crucifige," Luke 23:21.</note>

### The Day-Spring in its Rising.

always and eternally in the dark dungeon of Wrath <term>Grimmigkeit: In Böhme’s system, this refers to the "fierceness" or "wrath" of God’s nature—a dark, consuming fire that exists before it is tempered by the light of love.</term>.

Therefore I wish to have the reader faithfully warned, that they read this book with diligence and do not take offense at the simplicity of the Author <note>original Latin: "Authoris"</note>; for God does not look upon that which is high—since HE alone is High—but rather He looks to how He may help the lowly. If you reach the point where you grasp the spirit and mind of the Author, then no further admonition will be necessary; instead, you will rejoice and be glad in this light, and your soul shall laugh and triumph within it.

->Now observe:<-

Gracious Love, which is the fifth source-spirit <term>Quellgeist: Böhme’s term for the seven fundamental forces or properties that flow through both the divine nature and the created world.</term> in the divine power, is the hidden source which the corporeal <note>original: "corporliche"</note> being cannot grasp nor encompass, except only when it rises within the body <note>original Latin: "corpus"</note>; then the body triumphs within it, and conducts itself in a friendly and lovely manner. For it does not belong to the formation of a body, but rather rises up within the body like a flower out of the earth.

Now, this same source-spirit initially takes its origin from the sweet quality <term>Qualität: Böhme uses this to describe the distinct characteristics or "tastes" of the spiritual forces—such as sweet, bitter, or sour—that make up all existence.</term> of the Water.

Understand this as it is; mark it well here.

First there is the astringent <note>The German "herbe" implies a harsh, sour, or binding quality.</note> quality, thereafter the sweet, and after that the bitter; the sweet is located in the middle, between the astringent and the bitter. Now, the astringent always makes [things] hard, cold, and dark; and the bitter tears, drives, rages, and cuts; these two qualities rub and drive one another so harshly, and boil—

...so strongly that they generate heat; this is now dark within the two qualities, just like the heat inside a stone.

When one takes a stone, or something else hard, and rubs it against wood, both things become hot. Now, that same heat is merely a darkness, and there is no light within it; so it is also within the divine power. Now the harsh <term>herbe qualität: The first of Böhme's seven properties, representing contraction, coldness, and hardness—the "astringent" force that gives things form.</term> and the bitter quality, without the sweet water, rub and drive each other so hard that they generate the dark heat and ignite it within themselves.

And that, taken together, is the Wrath of God, the source and origin of the hellish fire. This can be seen in the case of Lucifer, who exalted himself and compressed himself so hard with his legions that the sweet source-water dried up within him—the very water in which the light is ignited and in which love rises. Therefore, he is now eternally a harsh, hard, cold, bitter, hot, sour, and stinking fountain; for when the sweet quality dried up within him, it became a dark, sour stench and a valley of misery <term>Jammerthal: A traditional German term for the "vale of tears" or a place of deep suffering, used here by Böhme to describe the internal state of a fallen being.</term>, and a house of corruption and wretchedness.

### Now further into the Depth.

When the harsh and the bitter quality rub against each other so hard that they generate heat, the sweet quality—the sweet source-water—sits right in the middle between the harsh and the bitter. Thus, the heat is born between the harsh and the bitter qualities within the sweet source-water, created by the friction of the harsh and the bitter qualities.

### Question.

### Answer.

This is the other open gate of the Godhead; here you may open your eyes wide and awaken the spirit in your half-dead heart, for this is no <term>conceit: "Dünckel," referring to a vain or unfounded human opinion or intellectual pride.</term>, fiction, or fantasy.

# Chapter 10.
## Concerning the Sixth Source-Spirit in the Divine Power.

The sixth <term>source-spirit: "Quell-Geist," one of the seven fundamental forces that flow from the divine essence.</term> in the divine power is **sound** or **tone**, such that everything resounds and rings within it; from this, speech and the distinction of all things follow, along with the ringing and song of the holy angels; and within it stands the formation of all colors and beauty, as well as the heavenly realm of joy.

Now you ask: What is tone and sound, or how does this spirit take its source and origin?

->Note:<-

All seven spirits of God are born within one another; one always gives birth to the other. None is the first, and none is the last; for the last gives birth to the first just as much as the first gives birth to the second, third, fourth, and so on to the last. However, that one is called the first and another the second, and so forth, is considered in light of which one is first in the <note>original: "bildung einer Creatur." This refers to the sequential logic of how a created being is structured or formed.</note> building and shaping of a creature. For all seven are equally eternal, and none has a beginning or an end. And because these seven <term>qualities: The fundamental properties of existence in Böhme's thought.</term> always give birth to one another, and none exists outside of the others, it follows that there is **One Single, Eternal, Almighty GOD**.

For if something were outside of or within the divine es-

sence is born, then it is not formed by one spirit alone, but by all seven: and if a creature, which exists as the whole being of God, <note>Böhme argues that because humans and angels are made in God's image, they contain the same seven fundamental forces (spirits) as the Creator.</note> corrupts itself in one source-spirit, elevating and igniting itself, then it ignites not just one spirit, but all seven.

The tone or <term>Mercury: original "Marcurius." In Böhme's mystical chemistry, Mercury represents the principle of sound, movement, and the "word" that brings internal qualities into external expression.</term> takes its origin in the first, that is, in the harsh and hard quality.

## Observe within the Depth.

Hardness is the fountainhead of sound; however, it cannot give birth to it alone, but rather it is the father of it, and the whole <term>Salitter: A term Böhme coined to describe the "divine saltpeter" or the celestial substance/soil of the kingdom of heaven.</term> is the mother; otherwise, if hardness were both father and mother of sound, then a hard stone would also have to ring. But as it is, a stone only clatters and knocks, appearing only as a seed or the beginning of sound; and of this it is certainly certain. But the sound or voice rises in the middle center in the **flash**, where the light is born out of the heat, where the flash of life arises.

## Observe how this happens.

When the harsh quality rubs itself against the bitter one, so that the heat rises in the sweet source-water, then the heat ignites the sweet source-water like a <term>flash: original "Pliz" (Blitz). This refers to the sudden ignition of divine light and consciousness.</term>; and this same flash is the light, the

## Here note the birth of the bitter quality even more clearly.

The origin of the **bitter quality** <note>original: "bittern qualität." In Böhme’s system, this is the third "source-spirit," representing the stirring, mobile, and often painful friction of life.</note> is this: when the **Flash of Life** <note>original: "Plitz des Lebens."</note> rises up in the heat into the **harsh quality** <note>original: "herbe qualität." The first quality, which is cold and contracting.</note>; and when the flash of fire, mixed with the water, enters into the harsh quality, the spirit of the fiery flash captures the harsh and hard spirit. These two together are a zealous, severe, and fierce source that rages and tears violently, much like a fiery, severe fierceness. I can compare it to nothing other than a **thunderclap** <note>original: "Donnerschlag."</note>, when the fierce fire first strikes down so that one’s sight fails; that same fierce fire is the nature of the **union** <note>original: "conjunction."</note> of these two.

->Nun mercke:<-
->Now note:<-

When this fire-spirit and the harsh spirit struggle against each other, the harsh one creates a severe, hard, and cold **astringency** <note>original: "Herbigkeit."</note>, while the fiery one creates a terrifying, fierce heat. Now, the rising up of the heat and the harshness creates a trembling, fierce, and terrifying spirit that rages and storms as if it wished to **tear the Godhead asunder** <note>original: "Gottheit zutrennen."</note>.

Then its fierce source transforms into a trembling, bitter, and greenish color, like a greenish darkness, and it retains

<meta>This page continues the discussion on how the human mind interacts with the seven divine source-spirits, emphasizing that man cannot comprehend all seven simultaneously due to his finite, mortal nature. Böhme describes the cognitive process as a "rising" of specific qualities within the "well-spring" of the heart.</meta>

<page-num>182</page-num>
<header>The Day-Spring in its Rising.</header>

...I cannot bear all seven of them at once in their perfection within my **corrupted brain** <note>original: "verderbten Gehirne." Böhme frequently humbles himself, suggesting that his physical, mortal mind is "corrupted" by the Fall and thus limited in its ability to process the full magnitude of divine light.</note>. I see all seven well enough, but when I **speculate** <note>original: "speculire." In 17th-century mystical German, "speculation" referred to a deep, meditative contemplation or a "mirroring" of the divine reality.</note> into them, the spirit rises in the central **well-spring** <note>original: "Quellbrunne."</note> where the spirit of life is born; it then rises either above or below itself and cannot grasp the **Spirits of God** <term>Geister Gottes: the seven fundamental qualities or energies of the divine nature that Böhme believes constitute all of reality</term>, all seven in a single thought or all at once, but rather piece by piece.

Each spirit has its own source, even though it is born from the others. Such is also the **comprehension** <note>original: "Begreifflichkeit." This refers to the human capacity to grasp or contain spiritual truths.</note> of man: he certainly has the well-spring of all seven spirits within himself, but whichever source the spirit rises in—those source-spirits in which that same spirit is most strongly formed—he grasps most sharply in that same rising. For even in the divine power, a spirit in its rising does not pass through all seven spirits at once. When it rises, it indeed stirs all seven at once, but it is caught in its rising, so that it must lay down its splendor and not triumph over all seven.

<insert>This is the nature of the senses and thoughts; otherwise, if a thought could pass through all forms through the **Center of Nature** <note>original: "Centrum Naturæ." ->Note this here:<-

Light a piece of wood / and you will see the mystery / the fire ignites in the hardness of the wood / which is the harsh, hard source / the source of **Saturn** <term>Saturn: In Böhme's "spiritual chemistry," Saturn is the first property of nature, representing the cold, astringent force that contracts and creates hardness or "matter."</term> / which makes the wood hard and tough. But the light—that is, the flash <note>original: "Pliz," an archaic form of *Blitz* (lightning), used here to describe the sudden eruption of light from fire.</note>—does not consist in the hardness / otherwise a stone would also burn / rather, the light consists in the sap of the wood / that is, in the water. Because there is sap in the wood / the fire shines as a radiant light / but when the sap in the wood is consumed / the radiant light is extinguished / and the wood becomes a glowing coal.

Now see / the fierceness <note>original: "Grimmigkeit," often used by Böhme to describe the "wrathful" or intense energy of the fire-principle before it is softened by light.</note> / which rises up in the light / does not consist in the water of the wood / but rather / when the heat rises up in the hardness / the flash is born / which is first caught by the sap in the wood / whereby the water becomes shining. But the fierceness or bitterness is born in the midst of the hardness and the heat in the flash / and therein it also consists: and as far as the flash reaches / that is, the fire's blaze <note>original: "Lohe"</note> / so far also reaches the fierceness of the bitterness / which is the son of hardness and heat.

But you should know this mystery / that the bitterness is already in the wood beforehand / otherwise the fierce bitterness would not be born so suddenly in the natural fire.

When it proceeds from the hardness and heat, the hardness knocks in the flash, and the heat rings out, and the light in the flash makes the sound bright, and the water makes it gentle; and in the harshness <note>original: "Herbigkeit," the astringent or tart property.</note> or hardness it is caught and dried up, so that it is a corporeal spirit in all qualities. — For every spirit among the **seven spirits of God** <term>Seven spirits of God: In Böhme's cosmology, these are the seven fundamental properties or qualities of nature (such as Sourness, Sweetness, Bitterness, Heat, etc.) that interact to create all reality.</term> is pregnant with all seven spirits of God, and they are all in one another like one spirit; none is outside of the other. Only such a birth takes place within them, and thus one brings forth the other, in and through itself, and the birth lasts from eternity to eternity in this way.

Here I wish to have the reader admonished that he should rightly consider the divine birth: You should not think that one spirit stands beside the other, as you see the stars in the sky standing next to each other; instead, they are all seven within one another like one spirit, as you can see this in a human being, who has various thoughts on account of the working of the seven spirits of God,

-># Aurora: The Day-Spring in the Ascent<-
->Hh 4<-

What humans need most is to take hold of the right faith; for when one grasps it, they penetrate through the wrath of God <note>In Böhme’s system, the "Wrath of God" is the dark, fiery foundation of all existence which must be tempered by light and love.</note> and through death into life, and they reign with God.

But because people now, at the end of this time, are very lustful for the root of the tree—through which nature indicates that the time of the tree's exposure is at hand—the Spirit wishes to show it to them and reveal the Godhead entirely. This is the **MORNING GLOW** <note>original: "MORGEN-RÖHTE," also known as Aurora, the title of this work.</note> and the dawning of the great Day of God, in which all that was born out of death into the rebirth of life shall be restored and shall rise again.

Behold, when God said, "Let there be Light," the light rose within the powers of nature or the seven spirits of God <note>The "Seven Spirits" are Böhme's core qualities of nature (sour, sweet, bitter, etc.) that drive all creation.</note>. And the firmament of heaven—which stands in the Word in the heart of the water—was enclosed between the sidereal <note>pertaining to the stars and the astral influences on the physical world</note> and the outermost birth by the Word and the heart of the water. The sidereal birth is the <term>place</term> <note>original: "locus"</note> of the dividing goal, which stands half in heaven and half in the wrath.

For from that same half-part of the wrath, the "dead birth" is now always born; and from the other half—which reaches with its innermost degree into the innermost Heart and Light of God—life is now always born through death. Yet the sidereal birth is not two bodies, but one.

However, when the creation of heaven and earth was performed in two days, and the heaven in the heart of the water became a distinc- <note>The text cuts off here at the catchword "scheid," which likely completes the word "Unterscheid" or "distinction."</note>

<summary>Böhme describes the "Morning Glow" as a divine revelation occurring at the end of time, explaining how the sidereal world exists as a tension between God’s wrath and His light.</summary>

-># The Day-Spring in the Ascent.<-

...gh the distinction <note>original: "Unterscheid." This completes the sentence from the previous page: "through the distinction..."</note> between the Light of God and the Wrath of God was made: so on the third day, through the terror <note>original: "schrack," a sudden shock, trembling, or awakening of power</note> of the fire-flash <note>original: "Feuerplitz," the sudden, lightning-like ignition of divine energy</note>, which rose up in the heart of the water, and which forced its way through death—remaining incomprehensible to death itself—all kinds of forms appeared once again, just as had happened before the time of the ignited wrath.

But because the water—which is the spirit of the sidereal <note>pertaining to the stars and the astral influences that govern the physical world</note> life—stood in the midst of the wrath and also in death, every body shaped itself according to how the birth into life and movement was.

## Concerning the Earth.

The earth was now the <term>Salitter</term> <note>Böhme’s unique term for the divine "saltpeter" or the fundamental substance of the heavenly world, which here has become "dead" and hardened due to the presence of divine wrath.</note>, which had been spat out from the innermost birth and stood in death: but when the fire-flash rose through the Word in the water, it was a terror; from this arose movement within death, and this same movement in all seven spirits <note>Böhme’s core qualities of nature—such as the sour, sweet, and bitter—which drive all existence.</note> is now the sidereal birth.

# The Depth.
### Understand this correctly.

When the fire-flash ignited itself in the water of death on the third day, life penetrated through the dead body of the water and the earth.

But now, the dead water and earth grasp nothing more than the flash or terror of the fire, through which their movement arises; however, the light—which rises quite gently within the fire-flash—neither the earth nor the dead water can grasp it.

<summary>Böhme explains the third day of creation, where a divine "fire-flash" animates the "dead" elements of earth and water, creating the sidereal life of the stars and nature. He distinguishes between the physical movement caused by this energy and the gentle light of God, which remains ungraspable by material things.</summary>

-># The Day-Spring in the Ascent.<-

It maintains its seat, however, in the kernel—which is the "fatness" <note>original: "fette," referring to the rich essence or fertile "oil" of divine life</note>, or the Water of Life, or Heaven—for it is the Body of Life that death cannot seize, yet it rises up within death. Likewise, the Wrath cannot seize it; instead, the Wrath remains in the terror <note>original: "schrack," a sudden shock, trembling, or awakening of power</note> of the fire-flash and creates the movement within the dead bodies of the earth and the water.

The Light, however, penetrates quite gently afterward and forms the "birth" <note>meaning the physical manifestation or offspring of nature</note>, which received its unified, corporeal body through the terror of the fire-flash.

### The Growths of the Earth.

Now, when the angry fire-flash awakens and mobilizes the spirits of nature—which stand in death within the earth—with its grim terror, these spirits begin to "bear themselves" <note>original: "sich zu gebären," to manifest or bring themselves forth into being</note> according to their own innate divine right, just as they have done from eternity. They shape a body together according to the qualities inherent in that specific place.

Whatever kind of <term>Salitter</term> <note>Böhme’s term for the fundamental "divine matter" or "saltpeter of the earth," the chemical-spiritual substrate that contains all the powers of nature.</note> died in death during the time the Wrath was ignited, and in whatever way it was working <note>original: "qualificirend," how the specific qualities and powers of the seven spirits were interacting</note> in the life of the seven spirits of God at that time: that is exactly how it rose up again in the fire-flash during the time of rebirth. It has become nothing "new," but merely another form of the body that exists in a state of "comprehensibility" <note>original: "Begreifflichkeit," referring to physical tangibility, materiality, or that which can be grasped by the senses</note> within death.

But now, the <term>Salitter</term> of the earth and the water is no longer able, in its dead state...

### Of the Metals in the Earth.

It is the very same substance and birth with the metals as with the growths above the earth. For the metal or ore, at the time the Wrath was ignited, stood within the turning wheel of the seven spirits of nature in the operation of Love, where, behind the fire-flash, the gentle benevolence is born. Therein stands the holy Heaven, which, in this birth—when Love becomes the <term>Primus</term> <note>original: "Primus," the first or chief principle</note>—manifests itself in such gracious clarity and beautiful colors, like gold, silver, and precious stones.

Since it was the most beautiful rising and birthing within holy, heavenly nature, it is also loved by mankind above all other things in this world. Therefore you also find ore, stones, water, and earth according to the quality of each spirit in the earth / and for that reason the earth has so many and various qualities, all according to how each <term>fountain-spirit</term> with its inherent birth was the <term>Primary</term> at the time of the ignition.

-># The Day-Spring in its Rising.<-

### Chapter 23.

#### Concerning the Depth Above the Earth.

When man looks at the depth <note>original: "Tieffe." This refers to the vast, three-dimensional expanse of the cosmos or the "Deep."</note> above the earth / he sees nothing but stars and water-clouds / then he thinks / it must be another place / where the Deity reveals itself with the Heavenly and Angelic government <term>Regiment: Böhme uses this word to describe the organized system, rule, or "dominion" by which God manages the universe through the stars and elements.</term>. He wants simply to distinguish the depth / together with its government from the Deity / for he sees nothing there but stars / and the government between them is fire / air and water.

Then he thinks / God made that from his purpose out of **NOTHING** <note>original: "NICHTS." Böhme is addressing the theological concept of *creatio ex nihilo* (creation out of nothing), arguing instead that the universe is formed from God’s own substance.</note> in such a way / how could God be in that essence / or how could that be God himself? He always imagines / it is only just a house / in which God rules and dwells with his Spirit: **GOD** could surely not be a God / whose essence consists in the power of this government.

Many a person might well say / What kind of God would that be / whose body / essence and power stood in fire / air / water and earth!

# 497

-># The Day-Spring in its Rising.<-

...power, blessing, food and drink come to you constantly from Him; likewise, all your knowledge <note>original: "Wissenschafft." Now, if there were a foreign god who exists outside of this one, who then would make you alive again out of this God in whom you have decomposed? How would that foreign god—from whom you were not created and in whom you have never lived—figure <term>figuriren: To shape, form, or reconstruct a physical structure; Böhme uses this to describe the "re-forming" of the body in the resurrection.</term> your body and spirit back together again?

If you are now a different matter <note>original: "materia." If His deity were now a different essence than His body, then there would have to be two kinds of deity in Him; His body would be from the god of this world, and His heart would be from the unknown God.

Oh, open the eyes of your spirit, you child of man! I will show you here the right and true, actual gates of the deity, just as that same One GOD wills it to be!

This implies a created being that remains an organic part of its creator, rather than a separate product.</note> out of Him and in Him—otherwise you would be nothing.

# 498

-># The Day-Spring in its Rising.<-

And see, and note the distinction of how all this is; for I do not write in a heathenly manner <note>original: "Heydnisch." Böhme is defending himself against charges of pantheism—the belief that the universe and God are identical. He argues his view is "philosophical," meaning it explores the mechanics of how the divine spirit inhabits material form.</note>, but philosophically; nor am I a heathen, but I have the deep and true knowledge of the one great God, who is All.

When you look at the Deep, the stars, the elements, and the earth, you do not grasp the bright and clear Godhead with your physical eyes—even though God is indeed present there and within them—rather, you first see and grasp with your eyes death, and then the wrath of God, and the hellish fire.

But if you lift up your thoughts and contemplate where God may be, you grasp the <term>sidereal birth: From the Latin "sidus" (star). In Böhme’s cosmology, this refers to the "astral" level of reality where the conflicting forces of the universe—like heat and cold, or love and wrath—interact to create the physical world.</term>, where love and wrath surge against one another. However, when you draw faith from the God who rules in holiness within this governance, you break through the heavens and grasp GOD by His holy heart.

Now when this happens, you are as the whole of God is—He who is Himself heaven, earth, stars, and elements—and you also have such a governance <note>original: "Regiment." This refers to the internal "administration" or "ordering" of spiritual and physical forces within a person that mirrors the structure of the macrocosm.</note> within you, and you are such a person as the whole God is in the place <note>original Latin: "loco."</note> of this world.

->Now you say:<-

How shall I understand this? Surely the kingdom of God and the kingdom of hell or the devil are distinct from one another, and cannot be one body?

Also, the earth and stones are not God, nor are the heavens and the stars;

<summary>Böhme explains that while God is present in all things, physical eyes only perceive the "wrath" or material side of nature; one must use faith to "break through" to God's holy heart. He concludes by introducing a hypothetical skeptic's question about how God can be one with "low" matter like stones or the realm of the devil.</summary>

-># The Day-Spring in its Rising.<-

...nor are the elements; much less can a human being be God—otherwise, would he not be unable to be cast out by God?

Here I will now explain the foundation to you step by step; keep the question in mind.

### On the Sidereal Birth and the Birth of God.

Before the times of the created heaven, and the stars and elements, and before the creation of the angels, there was no such Wrath of God, nor any death, nor any devil, nor even earth or stones. Likewise, there were no stars; instead, the Godhead birthed itself in a fine, gentle, and lovely way, and shaped itself into images. These were corporealized according to the <term>source-spirits: Böhme’s "Quellgeister" are the seven fundamental qualities or "spirits" that flow like a fountain to form the essence of God and nature.</term> through their birthing, struggling, and rising. They also passed away again through their struggling and formed themselves into another shape, all according to how each source-spirit was **the first** <note>original: "Primus."</note>, as you can read earlier.

But take careful note here: The earnest and severe birth, from which the Wrath of God, Hell, and Death have come, has indeed been in God from eternity, but it was not "ignitable" or manifest. For the whole of **GOD** stands in seven **forms** <note>original: "Species." Böhme uses this Latin term to describe the seven "qualities" or "properties" of divine manifestation.</note> or sevenfold shapes or births; and if these births did not exist, then there would be no God, no life, no angel, and no creature.

And these same births have no beginning, but have birthed themselves thus from eternity; and according to this depth, God Himself does not know what **HE** is. <note>This refers to Böhme’s concept of the "Ungrund"—the groundless abyss of the divine will that exists before any self-awareness or "somethingness" begins.</note>

Yet I must, after the manner of creatures, set them down one after the other, otherwise you would not understand it / for the Godhead is like a wheel / with seven wheels made one within another / where one sees neither beginning nor end.

->Now note:<-

First is the <term>astringent quality: original "herbe qualität." This refers to a harsh, contracting, or "sour" force that gives form and solidity to spirit.</term> / which is always being born of the other six spirits / it is in itself hard / cold / sharp / similar to salt / and yet much sharper. For a creature cannot sufficiently grasp its sharpness / since in a creature it is not alone and by itself / but according to the nature of the ignited "hellish" quality, I know how it is. This astringent / sharp quality contracts / and holds the forms and images in the divine body / and dries them / so that they may consist.

The 2nd birth is the sweet water / which is also born out of all six spirits / for it is the <term>gentleness: original "Sanfftmuht." A quality of softness and meekness that balances the harshness of the first quality.</term> / which is born out of the other six / and presses itself out within the astringent birth / and always ignites the astringent one again / extinguishing and softening it / so that it cannot manifest its harshness / as it would certainly have the power to do in its own sharpness / apart from the water.

# Aurora: The Day-Spring in the Rising

When these four <term>births: "Gebärungen"—the active, generative processes by which the divine qualities manifest.</term> rub together in this way, the <term>Primary Heat: original Latin "Primus"</term> arises and ignites itself in the sweet water; then the Light immediately rises up.

->Understand this rightly.<-

When the Light ignites, the <term>Fire-flash: "Feuerschrack"—a sudden, startling burst of fire or light, similar to a crack of lightning or a spark from flint.</term> precedes it; just as when you strike a stone, you see the fire-flash first, and only then does the light take hold from within that flash.

Now the <term>Fire-flash</term> travels through the water by means of the <term>harsh quality: "herbe qualität"—the astringent, contracting force that provides structure.</term> and makes it mobile; but the Light is born in the water and becomes shining. It is an inconceivable, gentle, and lovely being, such that neither I nor any creature can sufficiently write or speak of it; instead, I only stammer like a child who wishes to learn to speak. <note>Böhme frequently uses the "stammering child" metaphor to emphasize that human language is inadequate for describing divine mysteries.</note>

This same Light is born in the midst of these four <term>species: "Species"—the distinct types or categories of the divine qualities.</term>, out of the "fatness" or essence of the sweet water, and it fills the entire body of this birth. It is such a gentle well-being, such a pleasant scent and taste, that I know of no comparison for it, except for when life is born in the midst of death, or as if a person sat in a great heat of fire and was suddenly pulled out and placed into such great <term>gentleness: "Sanfftmuht"—often translated as meekness or mildness; a central virtue in Böhme’s theology.</term>, where they had previously felt the pain of the fire, which now suddenly vanished as they were placed into such a gentle well-being.

Thus the birth of the four <term>species</term> is placed into such a gentle well-being when the Light rises within them.

# Aurora: The Day-Spring in the Rising

But you must understand me correctly here; I write in a <term>creaturely manner: "Creatürliche Art"—from the perspective of a created being who experiences time and change, rather than from God's eternal perspective.</term>, as if a human being had been the Devil's prisoner and was suddenly set out of the hellish fire into the Light of God.

For the Light has no beginning in the <term>birth: "Gebärung"—the ongoing, generative process of God’s self-manifestation.</term> of God, but has shone thus from eternity in that birth, and God Himself knows no beginning within it. Only the Spirit opens the <term>gates of hell</term> to you here, so that you may see how things stand within the devils and hell, and how it is within a human being when the divine Light is extinguished and they sit in God's wrath; then they live in such a birth, and in such anguish, pain, and woe.

I cannot prescribe this in any other form either, for I must write as if the birth of God had or took a beginning, as if it had come to be so. Yet I write here truly truthful and precious words, which the Spirit alone understands.

## Now Observe the Gates of God.

The Light, which now births itself out of the fire and becomes shining in the water, and fills the entire birth, and illuminates it, and softens it—that is the true Heart of God, or the Son of God. For He is always thus born out of the Father, and is a different <term>Person: "Person"—referring to the Trinitarian concept of the Son as distinct from the Father.</term> than the <term>qualities: "qualitäten"—the active properties or forces of the divine nature.</term> and births of the Father.

The seventh generation exists and retains its birth and formation in the Holy Spirit; when the same spirit goes through the sharp births, it goes with the tone, and forms and fashions all kinds of <term>figures: "Figuren"—shapes, patterns, or archetypal forms that populate the divine realm.</term>, all according to how the sharp births wrestle with one another.

For they constantly wrestle with one another in the birth like a <term>love-play: "Liebespiel"—a harmonious, joyful interaction between the divine forces, rather than a conflict.</term>, and according to how the birth rises up with colors and tastes, so too are the figures formed.

To be sure, it is written quite correctly, yet the essence of God consists in power, and only the Spirit perceives it—not the dead flesh <note>Böhme frequently warns that his writing cannot be understood through mere intellectual study ("the dead flesh"), but requires a spiritual awakening in the reader.</note>.

The second birth is now the Water, which takes its origin in the Body of Nature.

->Observe:<-

When the Light shines through the astringent, contracted Body of Nature and softens it, then the gentle well-being is born within the Body. At that point, the hard power becomes quite gentle and melts like ice before the sun, becoming thin, just like water in the air; yet the foundation of nature, of heavenly <term>comprehensibility: *Begreifflichkeit*. The quality of being tangible or "graspable" to the senses or the spirit.</term>, remains standing. For the astringent spirit and the fire-spirit hold it; and the gentle water, which melts from the Body of Nature upon the kindling of the Light, passes through the severe and earnest, cold and fiery birth, and it is entirely sweet and lovely.

From this, the earnest and severe birth is now refreshed; and when it tastes it, it becomes elevated and rejoices—it is a rising-up of joy, wherein the life of gentleness is born. For this is the **Water of Life**, in which love in **GOD**, as well as in angels and men, is

In Böhme's time, "infecting" or "infusing" meant the way a scent, dye, or spiritual quality permeates a substance.</term> with the Water of Life—that same <term>shock: *Schrack*. A pivotal term for Böhme, referring to the sudden "startle," "crack," or "flash" of movement that happens when the dark, harsh forces of nature are struck by Light.</term> retains its bitterness from the severe birth; for the original birth is entirely severe, cold, fiery, and <term>astringent: *herbe*. The harsh, contracting force that Böhme identifies as the first quality of nature.</term>

Then the trembling shock is enlightened with the **gentleness** of the Light, and it goes up and down in the birth, crosswise, above itself and below itself, like a <note>original: "Rad mit sieben Rådern ineinander gemacht." A reference to the complex vision of wheels within wheels in Ezekiel 1:16, used here to describe the seven "Source-Spirits" of nature moving in harmony.</note> **wheel with seven wheels made within one another**.

Thus you see how no single power is the first, nor is any the second, third, fourth, or last; rather, the last gives birth to the first just as much as the first to the last, and the middle one takes its origin from the last, first, second, third, and so on. <note>Böhme is describing a non-linear, organic system where all divine qualities—what he elsewhere calls the "Source-Spirits"—are mutually dependent and eternally generating one another in a circular fashion.</note>

You also see how nature cannot be distinguished from the powers of God, for it is all one body: The **Godhead**—that is, the holy power of the Heart of God—is born within nature; likewise, the **Holy Spirit** arises or proceeds from the Heart of the Light, through all the powers of the Father, continually going forth, figuring and shaping all things.

This birth is now divided into three distinctions, where each is a specific and whole thing, and yet none is separated from the others.

### The Gates of the Holy Trinity.

The entire birth—which is the heaven of all heavens, as well as this world which exists within the body of the whole, as well as the <term>place: *locus*</term> of the earth and all creatures, and wherever you can think or imagine—all that together is **GOD the Father**, who has neither beginning nor end. Wherever you think or imagine, even in the smallest circle you might choose, the entire birth of God is perfect, unceasing, and irresistible.

<summary>Jakob Böhme describes the organic, non-linear unity of the Trinity within the "Body of Nature," explaining how the Holy Spirit shapes all existence from the Heart of God. He defines God the Father as the all-encompassing reality that exists even in the smallest point of the universe.</summary>

# The Day-Spring in the Rising

If, however, the light has been extinguished in a creature or in a place, then the <term>stern birth: *strenge Geburt*</term> <note>Böhme uses "stern birth" to describe the raw, harsh, and conflicting qualities of nature—such as bitterness, heat, and cold—when they are not balanced or "sweetened" by the divine Light.</note> is present there, which otherwise lies hidden in the innermost core within the light. This is now one part.

The other part, or the second Person, is the **Light**, which is perpetually born out of all the powers, and in turn illuminates all the powers of the **Father**, and contains the <term>source-fountain: *Quell-Brunn*</term> of all powers.

It is distinguished from the **Father** as a specific Person for this reason: it cannot "grasp" the birth-process of the Father <note>The Light is a result of the Father's powers, but it cannot contain or limit the Father's infinite, raw potentiality.</note>, and yet it is the Father’s **Son**, who is eternally born from the Father. You have an example of this in all the fires kindled in this world; simply reflect upon them. <note>Böhme frequently uses the analogy of fire and light: the fire (Father) is the source, but the light (Son) is what makes the fire's presence beneficial and visible.</note>

And the **Father** loves this, His only-begotten **Son**, so very heartily because He is the light and the gentle well-being within His body, through whose power the **Father’s** joy and delight arise.

These are now two Persons; neither can seize, hold, or fully grasp the other, yet one is as great as the other; and if one did not exist, the other would not exist either.

Take note here, you Jews, Turks <note>In Böhme’s seventeenth-century context, "Turks" was a common term for Muslims.</note>, and Heathens, for this concerns you! Here the gates of **GOD** are opened to you; do not harden your hearts, for now is the favorable time. You are by no means forgotten by **GOD**; rather, if you turn back, the light and the Heart of God will rise within you like the bright sun.

I write such things in the power and perfect knowledge of the great God, and understand

# The Day-Spring in the Rising

...His will in this very well. For I live and am in Him, and I flourish with this work out of His root and stem; so it must also be. Only take heed: if you blind yourself, then there is no more help for you, and you must not say that you did not know. Arise! The day is breaking!

The third distinction, or the third Person in the essence of God, is the **surging Spirit**, who arises from the rising in the <term>terror-flash: *Schrack*</term> <note>Böhme uses the word *Schrack* (modern *Schreck*) to describe the sudden, violent transition or "crack" where the raw powers of nature are ignited into life and light.</note> where life is born. This Spirit now surges in all powers and is the Spirit of Life; and the powers cannot seize or grasp Him again. Instead, He kindles the powers and, through His surging, creates figures and images, forming them according to the manner in which the <term>wrestling birth: *ringende Geburt*</term> <note>This refers to the constant, energetic struggle of the different qualities of nature—like heat, cold, and bitterness—as they "wrestle" to bring forth a specific form or creature.</note> stands in each place.

And if you do not wish to be blind, you should know that the **Air** is that same Spirit; however, <note>original: "in loco"</note> in the place of this world, nature within the air is quite significantly ignited in the fire of wrath—which Lord Lucifer did—and the **Holy Spirit**, who is the Spirit of Gentleness, lies hidden within it in His heaven.

You need not ask, "Where is that same heaven?" It is in your heart; only unlock it, and here the key is shown to you.

Thus there is one **GOD**, and three distinct Persons in one another, and none can grasp or hold back the other, nor fathom the other's origin. Rather, the **Father** begets the **Son**, and the **Son** is the **Father’s** heart, and His love, and His light; He is an origin of joys and the beginning of all life.

## On the Great Simplicity of GOD

Listen here, <term>Squire Hans on his brown nag: *Juncker Hans im braunenn Rössel*</term> <note>A mocking nickname Böhme uses for the proud, worldly person who thinks they are on a high horse of intellectual superiority but is actually riding toward spiritual death.</note> / you who ride from heaven into hell / and out of hell even into death / wherein the sting of death lies / look at yourself here / you worldly-wise man who are stuffed full of evil cleverness.

## Concerning the Concorporation 
## of the Stars.

Now when the entire body of Nature in the vastness of this world was frozen, as if in **hard death**, and yet life was hidden within it, God moved the entire body of Nature of this world on the fourth day. ->### The Day-Spring in its Rising<-

On the third day, life broke through death. For then the light broke through the darkness and made the dead body of Nature green and movable.

For on the third day, the body of Nature was so severely distressed <note>original: *geängstet*. In Böhme's cosmic chemistry, this "anguish" or "constriction" is a necessary tension that precedes the ignition of light.</note> until the **fire of love** <note>original: *Liebefeur*.</note> ignited within death; and the **light of life** broke through the stiffened body of death and grew green out of death. However, on the third day it existed only in the **fire-terror** <note>original: *Feuerschrecke*. This describes a sudden, startling flash of ignition or the "trembling" movement that occurs when life first stirs within the dark, harsh qualities of matter.</note>, from which movement arose.

But on the fourth day, the light rose and made its seat in the house of death—though death cannot grasp it. Just as the <term>strict birth of God</term> <note>original: *strenge Geburt Gottes*. This refers to the harsh, energetic foundation of divine power that exists in the "inner core" of all things, providing the "fuel" for the light.</note>, which stands in the innermost core from which life arises, cannot grasp **gentleness** <note>original: *Sanfftmuth*. A central concept for Böhme, representing the loving, light-filled, and calm aspect of the Divine that tames the "fire."</note> nor the light of gentleness together with the spirit in that gentleness—so too the dead darkness of this world cannot grasp the light of nature, and no devil can grasp it either.

Rather, the light shines through death and has made for itself its **royal seat** in the midst of the house of death and the wrath of God; it gives birth to a new body of God out of the house of wrath, which exists eternally in the love of God. To the old, kindled thing in the **outermost birth** <note>original: *eußersten Geburt*. This refers to the external, physical world which is subject to corruption and cannot perceive the spiritual reality within it.</note>, this is incomprehensible.

->### The Day-Spring in its Rising<-

...stands [there], and does not <term>co-qualify</term> <note>original: *inqualiret*. A term Böhme adapted from the Latin *in-qualificare*, meaning to work or resonate together with a specific quality or power. Here, it means the darkness does not share the same nature or "vibration" as the Light.</note> with the Light of God: But I will show it to you in earthly parables, so that you might come a little into a deeper understanding.

Look at a tree; it has on the outside a hard, coarse **husk** <note>original: *Schale*. This can also be translated as "shell" or "peel."</note>; this is dead and stiffened, yet it is not entirely in death, but rather in a state of impotence <note>original: *Ohnmacht*. In Böhme's vocabulary, this refers to a state where the divine power is present but inactive or "faint," lacking the strength to manifest as light.</note>, and there is a distinction between it and the **body** which grows beneath the husk. The body, however, has its living power, and breaks through the withered **husk**, and brings forth many beautiful young bodies, all of which nevertheless stand in the old body.

But the **husk** is like a dead thing, and cannot grasp the life of the tree, but only hangs upon it, and is a covering of the tree, in which the worms nest, and thereby finally destroy the tree also.

In this same way is the whole house of this world; the outward darkness is the house of the wrath of God, wherein the devils dwell, and it is rightly the house of death, for the holy light of **GOD** has died away within it.

> [Understand: it has entered into its **Principle** <note>original: *Principium*. For Böhme, a "Principle" is one of the three fundamental "worlds" or states of being: the dark fire-world, the light-world, and the outer material world.</note>, and the outermost essence of God is to be regarded as if it were dead; and yet it lives in God, but in a different source <note>original: *Quelle*. In this context, it refers to a different "well-spring" or quality of existence.</note>.]

The body, however, of this great house, which lies hidden beneath the husk of darkness, is incomprehensible to the darkness; it is the house of life, wherein love and wrath wrestle with one another.

<summary>Böhme uses the metaphor of a tree's dead husk and living inner body to explain how the material world (the house of wrath) contains but cannot "grasp" the divine light (the house of life).</summary>
