# The Day-Spring in its Rising.

Now Love always breaks through the house of death and brings forth holy, heavenly branches in the great tree, which stand in the light. For they grow green through the husk of darkness, just as the branch [grows] through the husk of the tree, and they are one life with God.

And the Wrath also grows green in the house of darkness, and keeps many a noble branch imprisoned in death through its infection in the house of fierceness <note>original: *Grimmigkeit*. In Böhme's thought, this refers to the harsh, violent, and consuming quality of the first three properties of nature when they are not tempered by the Light.</note>.

This is now the sum or the content of the <term>sidereal birth</term> <note>original: *Siderische geburt*. This refers to the "astral" or "starry" birth—the intermediate realm of the heavens and stars that influences the material world.</note>, of which I will write here.

->Now the question arises:<-

## What are the stars, or from what have 
## they become?

They are the power of the seven <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: *Quellgeister*. These are the seven fundamental qualities or creative forces of God—such as "Astringency," "Sweetness," and "Bitterness"—which Böhme believes constitute the divine nature and the fabric of all reality.</note> of God; for when the wrath of God was ignited by the devils in this world, the whole house of this world in nature—or the outermost birth—became frozen as if in death, from which the earth and stones are [made]. But when that same hard dross <note>original: *Abraum*. Literally "rubble" or "waste material," referring to the heavy, material residue left behind after the spiritual collapse.</note> was driven together into a lump, the deep became clearer, but entirely dark, for the light within it had died away in the wrath.

Now, however, the Body of God in this world could not remain in death; instead, God moved Himself with His seven source-spirits toward birth.

But you must understand this high matter rightly. The Light of God, which is the Son of God, as well as the Holy Spirit, has not died away—

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "new birth" from page 527. It links the three days of Christ's rest in death to the first three days of creation, establishing a bridge between temporal and eternal time.</meta>

# Morning Glow in its Rising <note>original: *Morgenröhte im Auffgang*. This is the title of the work, often referred to by its Latin title, *Aurora*.</note>

For that long / and not one hour longer / did the newborn King and Grand Prince of this world / Jesus Christ / rest in death / and was born again into the light during those same three days of nature’s creation / so that this temporal time would once again be one with Eternal Time / and so that no day of death should stand between them: And so that the eternal love / and the newly-born love from the new body of nature / might be one Eternal Love / and so that there would be no distinction between the eternal love and the newly-born love / but rather that the newly-born love should reach into the very <term>essence</term> <note>original: *Wesen*. In Böhme's thought, this refers to the fundamental substance or "being-ness" of reality.</note> that has existed from eternity / and continues for itself into eternity.

Thus, the newly-born love / which has risen from the water of life in the light / within the stars and within the whole body of this world / is so joined with the eternal / beginningless love / that they are one heart and one spirit / which carries and sustains everything.

In this ignition of light within the stars and elements / the birth of nature has not, however, been completely transformed into holy <term>gentleness</term> <note>original: *Sanfftmuht*. This refers to the calm, sweet, and loving quality of the divine light that balances the "harsh" qualities of nature.</note> / as it was before the time of wrath / such that the birth of nature is now entirely holy and pure. No / instead, it remains in its sharpest / strictest / and most <term>anguished</term> <note>original: *ängstlichsten*. As noted previously, this describes the intense, labor-like tension of nature's forces.</note> birth / where the wrath of God flourishes without ceasing / just like the fire of hell.

For / if nature had completely and utterly transformed its sharp birth into love / according to heavenly right / then the devils would once more be in the holy seat of God.

<meta>This page continues Böhme's explanation of the "new birth." Having previously established how Christ’s resurrection bridges temporal and eternal time, he now explains how the divine light (the new birth) operates within the harsh, "frozen" structures of the physical world and the stars.</meta>

<page-num>529</page-num>
<header>Morning Glow in its Rising.</header>

You can also clearly see and understand this by observing the cruel heat and cold, as well as the <term>poison</term> <note>In Böhme’s view, "poison" or *Gifft* often refers to the aggressive, driving force of nature that can be either life-giving or destructive.</note> of bitterness and sourness in this world; all of this exists within the birth of the stars, in which the devils lie imprisoned.

The stars are only the ignition of the great house; for the whole house is frozen in death, just like the earth. For the outermost birth is dead and frozen, like the bark on a tree; but the <term>sidereal</term> <note>original: *Siderische*. This refers to the "astral" birth, or the influence of the constellations on the physical world.</note> birth is the body in which life arises within.

But nature is entirely <term>sharp</term> <note>original: *scharff*. This describes the harsh, rigorous, or abrasive quality of material existence before it is tempered by divine light.</note> in its body; yet the new birth, which rises in the water of life and pierces through death, makes it gentle. However, it cannot change the core of the sharp birth; instead, it is born out of it and keeps its holy new life for itself, piercing through the wrathful death—and the wrathful death does not comprehend it.

This love and wrath are now indeed one body, but the water of life is the heaven of distinction between them, so that love does not contain or comprehend the wrath, nor does the wrath contain the love. Instead, love rises in the water of life and takes into itself the power from the first and strict birth—the power which is in the light and is born out of the wrath—so that the new body is born out of the old. For the old body, which exists in the strict birth, belongs to the devil’s house, and the new one belongs to the Kingdom of Christ.

<header>Morning Glow in its Rising.</header>
<meta>catchword: ge-</meta>

->Now the question arises:<-

So then, are not all three Persons of the **GODHEAD** in the birth of gentleness in this world?

**Y**es, they are all three in this world in the full birth of love, gentleness, holiness, and unity, and they are always born in such substance and being as has happened from eternity.

Behold, **God** the Father speaks to the people of Israel at Mount Sinai, as he gave them the Law: "I am a jealous, zealous **GOD** to those who hate me." <note>Exodus 20:5, Deuteronomy 5:9. The German *eifferiger* can mean both jealous and zealous, reflecting God's intense, consuming fire.</note>

Now, however, you cannot make two Persons out of this one Father, who is both wrathful and also loving; rather, he is one single Father, who always gives birth to his dearly beloved Son, and from both of them the Holy Spirit always proceeds.

->Observe the depth in the <term>Center</term><note>original: *Centro*. Böhme uses this term to refer to the inner spiritual core or the dynamic point where life and divine qualities originate.</note>.<-

**T**he Father is the single Being, who is himself everything, and who has always given birth to his dearly beloved Son from eternity; and in both of them, the Holy Spirit has always arisen in the <term>flash</term><note>original: *Plitze* (modern: *Blitz*). In Böhme's system, the "flash" is the sudden ignition of light and life where the dark, harsh qualities of nature are transformed into the divine light of the Son.</note> where life is born.

Now, however, from the strict and first birth of the <term>Source-Spirits</term><note>original: *Quellgeister*. These are the seven fundamental qualities or forces that constitute Böhme's divine and natural architecture.</note> of the Father, in which zeal and wrath reside, the body of Nature has always become; in it stands the light of the Son, as the Father’s heart—incomprehensible to Nature.

For the light is in the middle of the birth, and is the place of life, where the gentle life of God...

<summary>Böhme explains the paradox of God's dual nature as both a wrathful judge and a loving father, arguing that these are not separate persons but aspects of one divine being. He introduces the concept of the Source-Spirits and the "flash" of life where divine light originates within the center of nature.</summary>

is born out of all the powers of the Father; and in that same <term>place</term><note>original: *loco*. Böhme often uses Latin terms for specific metaphysical locations or states.</note> the Holy Spirit proceeds from the Father and the Son.

Now, however, those same powers of the Father, which exist in the kindling of the Light, are the Holy Father, and the gentle Father, and the pure birth of God; and the Spirit rising up within them is the Holy Spirit; but the <term>harsh birth</term><note>original: *scharffe geburt*. In Böhme's thought, the "harsh" or "sharp" birth refers to the initial, rigorous forces of nature—like contraction and friction—that must exist before the Light can be struck.</note> is the Body, in which this holy life is always born.

But when the Light of God shines through this harsh birth, it becomes entirely gentle, and is like a sleeping person, in whom life is always moving, while the body rests quite peacefully.

In this Body of Nature, the kindling has now occurred; for from this Body the angels were also created. And if they had not raised themselves up and kindled themselves in their <term>pride</term><note>original: *Übermuht*. This implies a "going beyond" one's proper limit or state of mind.</note>, their body would have stood eternally in silent and incomprehensible gentleness, just as it does in the other principalities of the angels outside of this world; and their spirit would have been eternally born in their body of gentleness, just as the Holy Trinity is born in the Body of God. Their inborn spirit would have been one heart, will, and love with the Holy Trinity; for they were created into that state within the Body of God for the joy of the Godhead.

But Lord Lucifer wanted to be the most powerful God himself, and kindled his body, and awakened the harsh birth of God within it, and set himself against the light Heart of God, intending with—

For the earthly body that you carry is one body with the whole ignited body of this world, and your body <term>inter-operates</term><note>original: *inqualiret*. Just as the new body of this world is born in its heaven, so also your new human is born in its heaven; for it is all one heaven in which God dwells, and in which your new human dwells, and they cannot be separated from one another.

But if you are godless, then your birth is not capable of heaven, but of wrath, and remains in the other part of the sidereal birth, where the earnest and severe <term>fire-fountain</term><note>original: *Feuerquell*. The source of spiritual energy or "fire" which, when not mitigated by the light of Love, is experienced as the "wrath" of God or hell.</note> rises up; and you are sealed in death so long until you break through heaven and live with God.

But I find that the most cunning **Devil** is set against me; he will stir up mockers, who will say that I wish to search out the **DEITY** <note>original: *GOTTHEIT*. In Böhme's thought, the Deity is the absolute, unmanifest source of all being.</note> through my own delusion.

Yes, dear mocker, you are indeed an obedient son of the **Devil**; you may rightly mock the children of **GOD**. Could I ever fathom the **DEITY** so deeply by my own ability, if the **DEITY** did not ground itself within me? But do you also think that I am strong enough to offer resistance <note>meaning: resistance against the divine impulse to write or against the Devil's temptations</note>?

Yes, dear proud human, the **Deity** is an altogether gentle, simple, and quiet Being, and does not brood in the ground of Hell and of Death, but in His Heaven, where there is nothing but harmonious **Gentleness** <note>original: *Sanfftmuht*. This refers to the sweet, loving quality of the divine light that tempers the "fierceness" of nature.</note>; therefore, it would not be fitting for me to do such things [of my own accord].

But look, I have not brought this forth myself; rather, your own desire and high-flown pleasure have moved the **Deity** to reveal your heart's desire to you in the greatest simplicity and in the deepest depths, so that it may be a testimony against you and an announcement of the earnest **Day of God**. I say this as a word of the earnest **God**, which is born in the **Flash of Life** <note>original: *Plitz des Lebens*. This is Böhme's signature term for the sudden moment of spiritual enlightenment or the "spark" where divine light ignites within the darkness of nature.</note>.

## Chapter 25.

### Concerning the Entire Body of the Star-Birth: That is, the Whole Astrology, or the Entire Body of this World. <note>For Böhme, "Astrology" is not merely predicting the future, but a "spiritual physics"—the study of how the celestial powers (stars) operate within the "body" of the universe and the human soul.</note>

<meta>This page transitions from Böhme’s defense against personal critics to a comparison between his spiritual "knowledge" and the academic "Star-Art" (astrology/astronomy) of his time. He acknowledges the validity of scientific observation but claims to see the hidden spiritual root that scholars miss.</meta>

<page-num>536</page-num>
<header># Morning Glow in its Rising.</header>

The scholars and highly experienced masters of the **Star-Art** <note>original: *Sternen-Kunst*. In the 17th century, this term combined what we now call astronomy and astrology—the study of how celestial bodies influence earthly life.</note> have reached so high and deep in their understanding that they know the course and effect of the stars: what their conjunctions, infections, and the breaking-through of their powers signify and bring about. They understand how winds, rain, snow, and heat are stirred up by them, as well as good and evil, fortune and misfortune, and even life and death, and all the workings of this world.

This is indeed the true foundation, which I recognize in the spirit to be so; however, their knowledge stands only in the **house of death** <note>original: *Hause des Todes*. This refers to the external, material world that is subject to decay and perceived only through the physical senses.</note> in external comprehensibility and in the viewing of the eyes of the body, and the **root of this tree** has remained hidden from them until now.

It is also not my intention to write about the tree's branches or to overturn their knowledge; likewise, I do not build upon their foundation, but rather let their knowledge sit in its own seat, since I have not studied it. Instead, I write in the spirit of my **revelation** <note>original: *Erkantnis*. For Böhme, this is not "book learning" but an experiential, mystical understanding granted by the Divine.</note> concerning the tree's root, trunk, branches, and fruit—like a laborious servant of his Lord—to expose the entire tree of this world.

I do this not with the intention of bringing something new onto the path, for I have no command for that; rather, my knowledge stands in this **birth of the stars**, in the midst of where life is born and breaks through death, and where the **surging spirit** <note>original: *wallende Geist*. A key Böhmean term describing the boiling, active movement of the divine life-force as it manifests in nature.</note> arises and breaks through—and in its drive and surging, I also write.

I also know very well that the children of the

Those of the flesh will mock me and say that I should tend to my **calling** <note>original: *Beruffs*. Böhme was a master shoemaker; his critics argued he should stick to his trade rather than writing theology.</note> and leave these things alone, and rather concern myself more diligently with that which fills the belly for me and mine, and let those philosophize who have studied it and are called to do so.

With this **temptation** <note>original: *Anfechtung*. This refers to a deep spiritual trial or an assault on one's faith, often attributed to the devil in Lutheran thought.</note>, the devil has also given me many a blow, and even instilled this into my own blood, so that I often resolved to leave this aside; but my undertaking became too heavy for me. For when I thought of the belly <note>meaning: material needs and worldly survival</note> and resolved within myself to abandon this project, then the **gates of heaven** were bolted shut in my **understanding** <note>original: *Erkantnis*. For Böhme, this is a direct, mystical insight from God.</note>.

Then my soul became distressed, as if it were captured by the devil, whereby **reason** <note>original: *Vernunfft*. Böhme distinguishes between human logical reason, which he often calls "dead reason," and divine wisdom.</note> received many a blow, as if the body were to perish; and the Spirit did not cease until it broke through the dead reason once more, and burst open the gates of darkness, and regained its seat in its proper place, through which it then always received new life and power.

Through this I then understand that the Spirit must be tested through **cross and tribulation**; likewise, I have not lacked physical temptation, but have always had to stand in battle, so greatly has the devil been set against it.

But because I sense that my eternal **salvation** <note>original: *Heyl*.</note> depends upon it, and that through my relenting, the **gates of light** would be closed to me—which is indeed the fortress of my heaven, into which [my soul flees]...

stand / thus He brought forth the entire old body of this world once again into a properly formed body / in which life ruled in a divine manner / although in ignited wrath / yet it still had to exist according to the right of the Godhead / so that a new body could be born out of it / which would endure in holiness and purity in eternity.

He dismisses the attempts of scientists to measure the vastness of the universe as futile efforts to grasp the intangible.</summary>

<header>Morning Glow in its Rising.</header>

But the true heaven is everywhere in this current time until the Last Day / and the house of wrath of hell and death is also everywhere in this world now until the Last Day. But the dwelling of the devils is currently from the moon down to and into the earth / in the deep caverns and caves / especially where there are desolate and wild wildernesses / and where the earth is very stony and bitter.

Their royal dominion, however, is in the deep in the four corners of the <term>equinoctial circles</term> <note>original: *Æquinoct-circul*. Böhme is referring to the celestial equator and the points of the equinox, which he maps onto a mystical geography of demonic influence.</note> / of which I will write in another place / but here I want to show you / how the body of this world came to be / and how it still stands now / and how the governance within it functions.

The entire body of this world is just like a human body / for it is enclosed in its outermost circle by the stars or the risen powers of nature / and within the body govern the seven spirits of nature / and the heart of nature stands in the middle.

But the general stars are the wondrous proportion or variation of God / for when God created the stars / he created them from the rising of infinity / out of the old body of God which has now been ignited <note>The "igniting" refers to the entry of the quality of "wrath" into the material world following the fall of Lucifer.</note>.

For just as the <term>seven source-spirits</term> <note>original: *Quellgeister*. These are Böhme's seven fundamental natural forces (such as contraction, friction, and heat) that generate all existence.</note> of God had, before the time of wrath, <term>intermingled</term> <note>original: *inficiren*. While this looks like "infect," in Böhme’s chemistry-influenced language, it refers to the way different qualities permeate or "tinge" one another during the process of creation.</note> with one another through their rising into an infinite birth / from which so many kinds of figures and heavenly growths emerged / so also did the Holy God shape his old body of this corrupted na-

<meta>catchword: tur</meta>

<summary>Böhme explains that heaven, hell, and the physical world coexist spatially, though the "demonic" realm is concentrated in specific earthly and astronomical locations. He introduces the concept of the universe as a "body" governed by seven source-spirits, modeled after the divine anatomy.</summary>

...nature into as many and varied powers / as had ever stood in the birth of holiness.

### Understand this high matter correctly.

Every star has a special property / which you also see in the beauty or the blossoming of the earth / and the Creator rebuilt the old ignited body into so many and varied powers and made it alive again / so that through this old life in wrath / such a new life might be born within it through the closure of heaven / so that the new life would have all the power and effect / as the old had ever had before the times of wrath / and so that it could <term>co-qualify</term> <note>original: *inqualiren*. A technical term used by Böhme to describe how spiritual qualities or forces interact, permeate each other, and operate as a single unit.</note> with the pure deity outside of this world / and that it might be a Holy God with the deity outside of this world.

Also, this new birth was blossoming at the time of creation / if man had not corrupted it / through which nature was corrupted even more / and God cursed the field.

Because man reached for the fruit of the old body <note>Böhme refers here to the Fall of Man, where Adam and Eve chose the material, "ignited" reality over the spiritual "new birth."</note> / the fruit of the new body now remained hidden in his heaven / and man must now behold it with the new body / and cannot enjoy it with the natural body.

Of which I would well lust to eat / but I cannot reach it / for heaven is the closure between the old and the new body / therefore I must go without / until that [other] life / and give my animal body Mother Eve's <term>apples of wrath</term> <note>original: *Zorn-Aepffel*. This refers to the physical, earthly food of our fallen world, which Böhme contrasts with the spiritual "fruit" of the heavenly realm.</note> to eat.

<meta>catchword: Of</meta>

<summary>Böhme explains that the diversity of the stars was designed to allow a new, holy life to emerge from the "ignited" world of wrath, though humanity's fall has currently separated our physical bodies from this spiritual nourishment.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Böhme's mystical account of creation. Having previously described the separation of heaven as a "closure" between the spiritual and material realms, he now details the "ignition" of life within the physical world during the third and fourth days of the biblical Genesis.</meta>

<page-num>543</page-num>

<header>Morning Glow in its Rising.</header>

# Concerning the ignition of the Heart or Life of this world.

When God had brought the body of this world into a proper form <note>original: *formam*</note> in two days, and had made the heaven as a distinction between Love and Wrath, then on the third day, Love pressed through the heaven and through the Wrath. At that moment, the old body moved within death and labored in the anguish of birth.

For Love is heated; it ignited the fountain of fire, which rubbed itself against the harsh and cold quality of congealed death, until the harsh quality grew hot on the third day. From this, the mobility—or the harsh earth—became moved.

For everything stood in a Fire-Shock <term>Fire-Shock</term> <note>original: *Feuerschrack*. A signature term for Böhme, describing the sudden, terrifying flash of energy or "terror" that occurs when the light of life ignites within the dark, dormant forces of nature.</note> until the fourth day, when the light of the sun ignited. Then the whole body labored in the anguish of birth, like a woman in labor.

The harsh quality had been the embracer or the encloser of life. Within it, the heat now struggled in anguish, having been ignited by the Love of God. It pressed out the harsh quality as if it were a dead body, but the heat kept its seat in the central point of the body and broke through.

But as the light of the sun now ignited, the nearest circle around the sun stood in the Fire-Shock. Then the sun, or the light, became shining in the water, and the bitterness from the water rose up along with the Fire-Shock. But the light was hurrying so swiftly after it that it seized the Fire-Shock; there it remained standing as a prisoner and became corporeal.

<meta>catchword: Da- (Then)</meta>

<summary>Böhme describes the third and fourth days of creation as a violent yet holy process where Divine Love ignites the "frozen" material world, resulting in the birth of the sun and physical life.</summary>
<keywords>Creation, Divine Love, Wrath, Fire-Shock, Sun, Birth, Harshness, Bitterness, Jacob Böhme</keywords>

From this, in the first rotation, the planet **MARS** came into being, whose power stands in the bitter Fire-Shock <term>Fire-Shock</term> <note>original: *Feuerschrack*. As noted previously, this is the sudden, terrifying flash of energy when life ignites within nature’s forces.</note>; for he is a rager, a ranter, and a stormer like a Fire-Shock; moreover, he is hot and a poisonous enemy of nature, through whose rising and birth all kinds of poisonous, evil worms <note>original: *Würme*. In Böhme’s time, this term referred generally to any creeping, crawling, or verminous creatures, often associated with corruption or the "wrath" of nature.</note> have come to be on the earth.

But because the heat in the central point of the body was so powerfully great, it also pressed so far out from itself, and opened the chamber of death before its ignition of the light so far, that it (the Sun) is the largest star.

As soon as the light ignited in the heat, the heated place <note>original: *locus*</note> was caught in the light, and the body <note>original: *corpus*</note> of the sun could not become any larger. For the light softened the heat; then the body of the sun remained standing in the middle like a heart; for the light is the heart of nature, and not the heat.

->But here you must specifically note:<-

As far as the central point ignited itself, so large is the sun; for the **SUN** is nothing else than an ignited point in the body of nature.

You must not think that there is perhaps some other power involved than that which is everywhere in the whole depth of the body.

If the love of God through its heaven wanted to ignite the entire body of this world through heat, it would be just as bright everywhere as it is in the sun.

<summary>Böhme describes the birth of the planet Mars from the "Fire-Shock" and explains that the Sun is an ignited point within the body of nature, where light acts as a tempering heart that softens the raw heat of creation.</summary>

Mm 3

<meta>This page continues Böhme's astronomical and theological discussion regarding the nature of the cosmos. Having previously introduced the sun's "royal place," he now explicitly defends a heliocentric model—the idea that the Earth moves around the sun—while explaining the sun not as a solid object, but as a localized ignition of divine light.</meta>

### Morning Glow in its Rising. 550

This opinion is wrong; rather, the Earth turns around and runs with the other planets, as if in a wheel, around the Sun. The Earth does not remain standing in one place, but runs once a year around the Sun, as do the other planets beneath the Sun, except for Saturn and Jupiter, who cannot do so because of their wide orbit and great height, since they stand high above the Sun.

->Now the question arises:<-

### What then is the Sun, and the other planets, or how did they come to be?

They, the other planets, are distinct bodies which have their own bodily properties, and are not bound to any stationary place, but only to their own orbit in which they run. But the sun is not such a body; rather, it is an ignited <term>place</term> <note>original: *locus*. Böhme uses this Latin term to emphasize that the sun is a specific location or "point" in space rather than a solid, material sphere like a planet.</note> through the Light of God.

->Understand it correctly.<-

The place where the sun is, is just such a place as you might choose anywhere above the Earth. If God willed to ignite the light through the heat, then the whole world would be purely such a sun; for the same power in which the sun stands is everywhere. Before the <term>Time of Wrath</term> <note>Böhme refers to a primordial state before the fall or the "breaking" of nature, where the harsh, burning quality of fire (Wrath) was tempered by light.</note>, it was just as light everywhere in the <term>place</term> <note>original: *loco*, the Latin ablative form of *locus*.</note> of this world as the sun is, but not so unbearable.

For the heat was not as great as it is in the sun; therefore the light was also very gentle, and because of the cruel grimness— <note>The text cuts off here at the catchword *keit*, part of the word *Grimmigkeit* (grimness or fierceness).</note>

<summary>Böhme argues for a heliocentric universe where the Earth orbits the sun, describing the sun itself not as a solid body but as a specific location where God's light is concentrated and ignited.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Böhme’s exploration of the sun's unique nature. Having established on the previous page that the Earth moves around the sun, Böhme now clarifies that the sun is not God Himself, but a physical manifestation of divine light within the material world, serving as the sole source of illumination for all other celestial bodies.</meta>

### Morning Glow in its Rising. 551

<meta>Mm 4</meta>

...ness of the sun, the sun is distinguished from the <term>Gentleness of God</term> <note>original: *Sanfftmuht Gottes*. In Böhme’s theology, this is the "Light-world" or the second principle of the divine essence, which is peaceful and loving, as opposed to the "Wrathful" fire.</note>. Therefore one must not say that the sun is an open gate of God’s light; rather, it is like the light in a human’s eye, where the location of the eye belongs to the body, but the light is distinguished from the body.

And although it arises through the heat in the water of the body, it is nonetheless a distinct thing which the body cannot grasp; such a distinction also exists between God the Father and the Son.

Thus, on the fourth day, in the <term>Anguish-Birth</term> <note>original: *Angst-Geburt*. This refers to the difficult, contracting process of nature as it moves from dark, "hungry" desire toward the breakthrough of light.</note> of this world, at the central <term>point</term> <note>original: *Punct*.</note> of this world, the sun rose and stands still in its eternal, <term>bodily</term> <note>original: *corporlich*.</note> place; for it cannot take a place for itself and move to another.

For it is the only natural light of this world, and outside of it there is no longer any true light in the house of death: And although it seems as if the other stars also shine brightly, it is nonetheless nothing; rather, they all take their <term>luster</term> <note>original: *Glantz*. This implies reflected radiance or brilliance.</note> from the sun, as follows shortly hereafter.

The true birth and origin of the sun and planets, however, is constituted as follows:

When the heaven was made as a distinction between the Light of God and the ignited corruption of the body of this world, the body of this world was then a dark valley and had no light that would have shone in the external body outside of heaven; there stood

He asserts that the sun is the stationary, central source of all natural light, from which all other planets and stars derive their brilliance.</summary>

The Earth does not remain standing in one place, but runs around the Sun once in a year, as do the other planets beneath the Sun—except for Saturn and Jupiter, who cannot do so because of their wide orbits and great height, since they stand high above the Sun.

->Now the question arises:<-

### What then is the Sun, and the other planets, or how did they come to be?

Behold, the other planets are distinct bodies which have their own physical property and are not bound to any stationary place, but only to their orbits in which they run: The Sun, however, is no such body, but is a place <note>original: *locus*; Böhme uses the Latin term to emphasize a specific spatial coordinate or "opening" in the fabric of the universe.</note> ignited by the Light of God.

->Understand it correctly.<-

The place where the Sun is, that is such a place as any place you might choose above the Earth. If God willed to ignite the light through the heat, then the entire world would be nothing but such a Sun; for the same power in which the Sun stands is everywhere. And before the time of the Wrath <note>In Böhme's theology, the "Wrath" (*Zorn*) or "Grimmigkeit" refers to the harsh, fiery quality of the first principle of nature that became dominant following the fall of Lucifer; before this, the world existed in a state of gentle, pervasive light.</note> it was just as bright everywhere in the place <note>original: *loco*</note> of this world as the Sun is, but not so unbearable.

For the heat was not as great as in the Sun; therefore the light was also very gentle, and because of the cruel fierce-

# Morning Glow in its Rising.
Mm 4

[...severi]ty of the Sun, the Sun is distinguished from the **Gentleness of God**. For this reason, one must not say that the Sun is an open gate of God’s light; rather, it is like the light in a human eye. In that case, the location of the eye belongs to the body, yet the light is distinguished from the body.

And although it arises through the heat in the water of the body, it is nonetheless something distinct which the body cannot grasp; such a distinction also exists between God the Father and the Son. <note>Böhme uses the analogy of the eye to explain how the divine and the physical can occupy the same space without being the same substance. He frequently compares the Sun to the Father (the source/fire) and its light to the Son (the radiance/gentleness).</note>

Thus, on the fourth day, in the **birth-pang of anguish** <note>original: *Angst-Geburt*; Böhme’s technical term for the agonizing tension or "contraction" in nature that precedes the breakthrough of light and life.</note> of this world, the Sun rose at the center point of this world. It remains still in its eternal physical location, for it cannot take itself from one place and set itself in another.

For it is the only natural light of this world, and outside of it, there is no longer any true light in the **house of death**: <note>The "house of death" refers to the material world after its "corruption" or fall, which Böhme views as a dark valley requiring the Sun’s illumination to be visible or habitable.</note> And even though it seems as if the other stars also shine brightly, it is actually nothing; instead, they all take their radiance from the Sun, as follows shortly hereafter.

### The true birth and origin of the Sun and Planets, however, is constituted as follows:

When Heaven was made to distinguish between the **Light of God** and the ignited corruption of the body of this world, the body of this world was then a dark valley. It had no light that would have shone in the external body outside of Heaven; there stood all

# Morning Glow in its Rising.

...all powers, as if held captive in death, were so greatly anguished until they heated up in the center of the body.

But when this happened—that the **anguished birth** <note>original: *ängstliche Geburt*; Böhme uses this to describe the intense, labor-like tension of the natural elements before they are illuminated by spirit.</note> stood so intensely in the heat—the love in God’s light broke through the heaven of distinction <note>The "heaven of distinction" or "separation" refers to the boundary between the physical realm and the divine light.</note> and ignited the heat. Then, in the heat within the water—or in the oiliness of the water—the shining light rose up, and the heart of the water was ignited; and this took place in a single moment.

For as soon as the light truly seized the body <note>original: *corpus*</note>, the body was captured in the light, and the heat was captured and transformed into a moderate **gentleness** <note>original: *Sanfftmuht*; a key Böhmean term for the state of grace or the cooling, life-giving quality of the divine light that tempers the "fire" of existence.</note>, and could no longer remain in such anguish.

But because the heat was startled by the light, its fierce fire-source <note>original: *Feur-Quall*; a wordplay in Early Modern German suggesting both a "source" (*Quelle*) and "torment" (*Qual*).</note> subsided, and it could no longer ignite itself further. Furthermore, the breakthrough of love in God’s light through the heaven did not extend any further at this time, according to God’s decree; therefore, the sun did not grow any larger.

### The 26th Chapter.

## Concerning the Planet SATURN.

SATURN, the cold, sharp, and strictly harsh ruler / takes his beginning and origin not from the Sun / for he has in his power the chamber of death / and is a dryer-up of all powers / from which corporeality arises.

Just as the Sun is the heart of life / and an origin of all spirits in the body of this world, so Saturn is a beginner of all corporeality and comprehensibility <note>original: *Begreifflichkeit*; the state of being tangible or graspable by the senses</note> / and the entire body of this world stands in the power of these two planets / and no creature or formation / as well as no mobility / may come to be in the natural body of this world / outside the power of these two.

But his origin is the earnest, harsh, and strict anxiety of the whole body of this world; for when, at the time of the kindling of the wrath / the light in the outermost birth of this world was extinguished—which birth is physicality or comprehensibility / or the rising up of the birth of all <term>source-spirits <note>original: *Quellgeister*; Böhme’s term for the seven fundamental forces or qualities of nature that "well up" to create reality</note></term>—then the harsh quality stood in its sharpest and strictest birth / and contracted the workings of all source-spirits quite harshly and strictly together.

And just as in humans the skull is a container and encloser of the brain, within which thoughts are born, so too is the Saturnian power <note>The "Saturnian power" refers to the cosmic force of contraction and solidification that allows spiritual ideas to take on physical form</note> a container, a desiccator <note>one who dries or hardens</note>, and a sustainer of all corporeality and tangibility.

And just as the planet *Jupiter*, which is an opener and bringer-forth of gentleness, stands between the fierce *Mars* and the strict *Saturn*, and brings forth gentleness and wisdom in all creatures: so too is the life and the sense of all creatures born between these two qualities—primarily the new body of this world, as well as the new man, about whom you will find more in the description of mankind.

## Of the Planet *VENUS*.

*VENUS*, that lovely planet, or the kindler of love in nature, also has its origin and descent from the rising of the Sun; however, its quality, essence, and origin are constituted in this way:

->Note this rightly and properly.<-

When the Love of God kindled the place <note>original: *locum*</note> of the Sun, or the Sun itself, there first arose—out of the anxiety, out of the place of the Sun, and out of the seven source-spirits <term>Quellgeistern: the seven fundamental active qualities or "fountain-spirits" that Böhme believes drive all of creation</term> of nature—the terrifying and fierce fire-shriek <note>original: *Feurschrack*; in Böhme’s theosophy, this represents the sudden, violent flash or "crack" that occurs when light breaks through the harsh, dark forces of nature</note>, whose birth and initial origin is the kindled fierce wrath of **GOD**, within the harsh quality through the water.

This occurred first during the kindling of the Sun

out of the chamber of death, and was an awakener of death and a beginner of life; and it rose quite fiercely and trembling upwards until the light of the Sun seized and infused <note>original: *inficirete*; meaning to permeate with a quality or "infect" in a transformative way</note> it. ### The Gates of Love.

But when the two spirits of movement and of life had risen from the place <note>original: *loco*</note> of the sun through the ignition of the water, then gentleness—as a seed of the water with the power of light—pressed quite softly, infusing and friendly downward into the chamber of death; from this the love of life, or the planet **Venus**, was formed.

->You must, however, understand this high thing correctly here.<-

The birth or the rising of the seven planets and all the stars is nothing other than the way in which life and the wondrous proportion of the Godhead has been born from eternity.

For when King Lucifer had prepared the <term>place <note>original: *locum*</note></term> of this world for himself as a house of wrath, and intended to rule within it so fiercely and powerfully, then the light in nature—wherein he intended to be a lord—was immediately extinguished. The whole of nature became stiff and frozen, like a body of death in which there was no mobility, and he was forced to remain as an eternally-captive one in the darkness.

Now, however, Holy God did not want to leave this <term>place <note>original: *locum*</note></term> of His body—understand this as the space of this world—standing in eternal darkness and shame, nor leave it as the devils' own property; instead, He gave birth to a new regime of light, and of all seven <term>source-spirits <note>original: *Quellgeister*; Böhme’s term for the seven fundamental qualities or forces of divine nature that flow into one another like a fountain</note></term> of the Godhead, which the devil could neither seize nor grasp; it was also of no use to him.

For he can see no more in the light of the sun than in the darkness, for he did not become a creature within this light; therefore, it is of no use to him.

But because it was to be a new regime, it had to be a regime that the devil could not grasp, and which he could not use as his own physical property.

### This is now constituted as follows:

The Love, or the Word, or the Heart—that is, the only-begotten Son of God the Father, who is the light and the gentleness, and the love and the joy of the Godhead (as He Himself says, having taken humanity upon Himself: *I am the light of the world*, John 8:12).

...[has] taken the <term>place <note>original: *locum*</note></term> of this world by the heart; and in the middle of this space, in the spot where the mighty Prince and King Lucifer had sat before his fall—and where he had been made into a creature—it was born anew.

First, there arose the <term>fire-crack <note>original: *Feuerschrack*; Böhme’s term for the sudden, terrifying burst of movement or "shriek" that occurs when fire is ignited, representing the transition from stillness to life</note></term>, or the mobility within the heat; this is the beginning of life in the chamber of death. After that, 2. the light became shining within the <term>fatness of the water <note>original: *fetten des Wassers*; an alchemical reference to the "oil" or spiritual essence within matter that allows light to burn</note></term> in the heat; this is now the sun.

Thirdly, as the light of the sun has now <term>infected <note>original: *inficiret*; in this context, it means to permeate or transform the essence of something, rather than to cause disease</note></term> the entire <term>body <note>original: *corpus*</note></term> of the sun, the power of life—which arose from the first infection—ascended upward, just as when one ignites a piece of wood, or when one strikes fire from a stone.

Thus one sees first the brilliance, and from the brilliance the fire-crack, and after the fire-crack the power of the ignited <term>body <note>original: *corpus*</note></term>; and the light, along with the power of the body, suddenly raises itself above the crack and reigns much higher, deeper, and more powerfully than the fire-crack.

Also, the power of the ignited <term>body <note>original: *corpus*</note></term> operates within the arisen power outside the fire—softly, lovely, and ingeniously—and herein one rightly understands the divine being. It also has a form [consistent] with the becoming of the sun and the two planets, Mars and Jupiter.

Because the <term>place <note>original: *locus*</note></term> of the Sun—that is, the Sun itself—contained all qualities according to the Divine Right, as well as all other places within itself, all qualities immediately rose and descended during the first ignition, and they bore themselves according to the eternal, beginningless Law.

For the power of the Light—which softened the harsh and bitter qualities in the <term>place <note>original: *loco*</note></term> of the Sun and made them thin like water, or like the Love of Life—descended downward according to the nature of humility.

From this, the planet Venus came to be; for in the House of Death, she is an opener of gentleness, or an igniter of the water, and a soft penetrator through hardness—an igniter of Love, in which the upper dominion (namely, the bitter heat of Mars and the heartfelt sensibility of Jupiter) becomes full of longing.

From this arises the <term>infection <note>original: *inficirung*; in Böhme’s vocabulary, this refers to a transformative permeation or "tinting" where one quality influences another, rather than a biological disease</note></term>; for the power of Venus makes the fierce Mars—or <term>fire-crack <note>original: *Feuerschrack*; the sudden burst of energy when fire is lit</note></term>—mild and softens him, and she makes Jupiter humble. Otherwise, the power of Jupiter would break through the hard <term>chamber of Saturn <note>Saturn represents the contractive, cooling, and "containing" force in Böhme’s system</note></term>, and through the skulls of humans and animals, and sensibility would be transformed into pride against the Godhead’s Right of Birth, in the manner and way of the proud Devil.

### Of Man and the Stars.

Just as the depth / or the house of this world is a dark house / where corporality is born entirely thick / dark / anxious and half-dead / and takes its governance from the planets and stars / which ignite the body in the outermost birth / from which the movement of the elements arises / so is the figurative and creaturely being / and so also the house of flesh <note>original: *Fleischhauß*; a term Böhme uses to describe the human body as a temporary dwelling for the soul.</note> of man is a dark valley / where indeed the anxiety for the birth of life is within / and always labors greatly / with the will to raise itself into the light / from which life might be ignited.

In Böhme's cosmology, the "Oil" of the heart represents the mild, receptive substance of life.</note> Oil of the heart begins, from which immediately a **Mass**, seed, or driving will of another human being arises in the **Mass**.

In just such a way did the first **Mass** also come to be; for the Spirit of Love in the Heart of God looked upon the water of life in the body of the ignited, wrathful Father; from this and out of this, love rose up in the flash of fire <note>original: *für der Zeit des Zorns*; against or in the presence of the time of wrath.</note> against the time of wrath.

In this gaze, one spirit captured the other; the Oil or water in the wrath received the Spirit of Love in the Heart of God, and <term>inter-qualified: *inqualiret*; a term Böhme uses to describe how different spiritual qualities or "source-spirits" merge and work within one another to produce a specific effect.</term> with it, and the harsh spirit contracted the **Mass**: there it was already a birth or a will of a whole **creature**, just like the seed in a human being.

But now the firmament of heaven has been closed between the Heart of God and the ignited, hard chamber of death; otherwise, life would have immediately ignited itself in the **Mass**. For the firmament was in the **Mass** as well as outside the **Mass**, which is the dividing boundary between the Heart of God and the fierce devils.

Therefore, the Word or Heart of God had to blow upon the surging spirit in the **Mass**, which only happened on the sixth day, for certain reasons.

If, however, heaven had not been closed as a firmament in the **Mass**, between the Heart of God and the corporal source-spirits of the **Mass**, then the **Mass** could have [brought forth] the soul out of its own...

<vocab>Massa (Mass), Liebegeist (Spirit of Love), Hertze Gottes (Heart of God), Feste des Himmels (Firmament of Heaven), Quellgeister (Source-spirits), Seele (Soul), Feurpliz (Fire-flash), inqualiret (inter-qualified)</vocab>

<summary>Böhme explains that the creation of the first human "Mass" resulted from the Heart of God's loving gaze meeting the wrathful elements of nature. He emphasizes that the Firmament acts as a necessary barrier to prevent the premature ignition of life within this material mass until the appointed time on the sixth day.</summary>

It was to be feared, however, that things would have gone as they did with the beautiful little son Lucifer, because the corporeal **source-spirits** <term>source-spirits: *Quellgeister*; Böhme’s term for the fundamental forces or "qualities" of nature that "well up" to create all life and matter.</term> in the **mass** <note>original: *Massa*; the raw, unformed substance of the first human.</note> were infected by the fire of wrath.

That precious man Moses writes that God made man out of a **clod of earth** <note>original: *Erdenkloos*; Böhme is referring to the German translation of Genesis 2:7, where man is formed from the dust of the ground.</note>, as the scholars have translated it into German; however, he was not present when it happened.

But I must say this: Moses certainly wrote rightly, yet the true understanding of what the earth was made from has remained hidden in the literal letter to both Moses and his descendants; the Spirit has kept it hidden until this time.

It was also hidden from **Adam** while he was still in Paradise; but now it becomes entirely manifest, for the Heart of God has applied itself to the chamber of death and is about to break through.

Therefore, from now on, more and more—

[the soul] hides / and there receives its own rays / from the light of God / it passes therefore through the firmament of heaven <note>original: *Feste des Himmels*; a spiritual boundary within the human being that separates earthly consciousness from the divine.</note> like heat lightning / yet quite gently / like a lovely delight.

Now come here, you choosers of the election of grace <note>original: *Gnaden-Wähler*; Böhme is addressing contemporary theologians who believed in predestination—the idea that God pre-determines who is saved or damned.</note> / you who imagine / you have hit the mark / and who consider simple faith to be a fool / you have danced before this door for a long time / and boasted of the Scripture / how **GOD** has chosen some people in their mother’s womb in grace for the Kingdom of Heaven / and rejected others.

<page-num>591</page-num>

This book, *Morning Redness* <note>original: *Morgenröhte*; better known today by its Latin title, *Aurora*.</note>, is the first part which the late Jacob Böhme, B. A. S. <note>original: *B. A. S.*; likely an abbreviation for *Bohemus Autodidaktus Silesius*, meaning "Böhme, the self-taught Silesian."</note>, brought to the light of day. However, at the instigation of the Head Pastor of Görlitz, Gregorius Richter, it was forcibly taken from him by an Honorable Council of that place. It lay there for 27 years, until the 26th of November in the year 1641, when through God’s special providence it came to the light of day once again through D. P. S., the Mayor of that city, who presented it to the Electoral Saxon House Marshal, Georg Pflug, in Dresden.

This book is a wonder of the world, which the holy soul will well understand.

---

# Concluding Reminder
## Of the
## Lovers of this Book.

True-hearted reader, up to this point, the fundamental-writing hand of our author—awakened by God—has given the fullest possible treatment of the seven-fold wonder-wheel of the unfathomable *Ungrund* <term>Ungrund: Böhme’s signature term for the "groundless" or "abyssal" divine void; the nothingness out of which the will of God and all existence emerges.</term> of the mind of eternity. At this place, he has been held back <note>The sentence ends abruptly due to the original text being interrupted or the author being prevented from continuing.</note> by the resistance of the good, as the damned originator of wrath—

<summary>This page provides a historical account of how Böhme’s first manuscript was suppressed by church authorities in Görlitz for 27 years before its rediscovery. It also begins a mystical postscript regarding the divine "Ungrund" and the spiritual "wonder-wheel."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the historical and theological postscript regarding Jacob Böhme’s silence and his subsequent works, following the account of the manuscript's suppression.</meta>

[and was compelled] not to proceed any further, but rather to maintain a seven-year silence due to the interference of his persecutors <note>After Böhme wrote *Aurora* in 1612, the local pastor Gregorius Richter successfully lobbied the city council to ban him from writing. Böhme remained silent until 1619.</note>; for otherwise, by his own admission, the present *Morning Redness* <note>original: *Morgenröhte*; the work also known as *Aurora*.</note> would have become much longer, and more would have been treated therein regarding the Creation. After the completion of these seven years, however, the spirit instilled in him by Divine Enlightenment impelled him to unfold his deep knowledge through further writing. From this then followed first the book *Of the Three Principles* <note>original: *Buch von den drey Principien* (1619).</note>, and after that *Of the Threefold Life* <note>original: *vom dreyfachen Leben* (1620).</note>, etc. These works so far surpass this one in their more perceptible content as the bright midday outshines the morning dawn. If the diligent reader should notice anything cumbersome or difficult to grasp in the present book, let him feel no aversion, but rather wait and long for its clarification in the aforementioned subsequent works. Alongside us and all the children of JESUS, may he join in the praise of the most high God in the following humble offering of praise:

The pomp of joy <note>The word *Pomp* (splendor or ceremony) here refers to the celestial glory inhabiting the soul.</note> dwells comfortably in him /
His wrath also crackles <note>original: *knallt*. Suggesting a sharp, terrifying sound like thunder or a whip, symbolizing divine judgment.</note> terribly against the wicked.

Whose city is the most beautiful Zion,
With gates of precious stones and pearls,
Built to the praise of **JESUS CHRIST**
For us, the chosen host;
We shout to Him with thanks, praise, honor, and fame,
In Zion’s city, the property of the Most High.

---

->These two following letters, composed by the late<-
->Mr. D. V. T. <note>Likely Johann Theodor von Tschesch (1595–1649), a Silesian nobleman and prominent student of Böhme’s writings.</note>, we have seen fit to share<-
->with the favorable reader from a well-meaning heart.<-

The *Aurora* <note>Böhme’s first work, written in 1612, also titled *The Root of the Morning Redness*. It was famously seized by city authorities in Görlitz.</note> of **Jacob Böhme**, as it seems to me, and as the context and the whole system <note>original: *Systema*. Referring to the cohesive theological and philosophical structure of Böhme's thought.</note> demonstrate, is not complete, for it ends with such a short account of the Creation and the mystery of man. Indeed, the late author himself later judged this writing as a still-imperfect birth, and always asked that one should pause with it as his first birth: because there are many things in it which could cause a contradiction with his other writings and make the whole work suspect.

<summary>This page concludes a hymn celebrating the spiritual Zion and introduces a critical commentary on Jacob Böhme’s first book, Aurora. The text explains that Böhme himself viewed this early work as an incomplete and potentially misleading "imperfect birth" compared to his later writings.</summary>

--- Page 611 ---
<meta>This page continues the editorial introduction of letters written by Hans Dieterich von Tschesch. The previous page established that while Jacob Böhme himself viewed his first work, *Aurora*, as an "imperfect birth," the writer of these letters holds it in much higher regard as a foundational text for the "true philosophy."</meta>

Although it [the book] seems to me very noble / and in a certain way / almost to be preferred over the others / and that / if all his books were lost / the whole full knowledge of the true Philosophy <note>original: *Philosophiæ*</note> could be restored from it. It is also more convenient and clearer for beginners / and a Compendium <note>A concise summary or handbook.</note> of the entire System <note>original: *Systematis*</note>.

->At Brieg <note>A city in Lower Silesia, modern-day Brzeg, Poland.</note>, December 6, Year 1641.<-
->H. D. V. T. <note>Hans Dieterich von Tschesch (1595–1649), a Silesian nobleman and student of Böhme’s theology.</note><-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
I consider / that in reading the writings of this Divinely learned man, the
<margin>2.</margin>
*Morning Redness in the Rising* <note>Böhme’s first work, *Aurora*, written in 1612.</note> could initially be taken in hand / as a Synopsis <note>original: *Synopsis*; a brief overview or summary.</note> and short
<margin>3.</margin>
concept of all his writings: out of which the beginning and knowledge of his writing flowed / and especially / because the Author was set in his first own naked gift <note>Referring to Böhme's initial divine illumination, which he claimed came to him directly from God before he began studying other scholars.</note> / before he had yet associated and conversed <note>original: *conversiret*</note> with other people / such as Dr. Balthasar Walther <note>A physician and chemist who introduced Böhme to Paracelsian terminology and helped disseminate his works.</note>. During which time he afterwards somewhat adapted himself to their ways of speaking / also adopting their phrases <note>original: *phrases*</note>, although he grasped them much more deeply. And then also / because therein he spoke / in my opinion / most clearly and
<margin>4.</margin>
simply to beginners / just as I was then able to find my way into it most easily at first / etc.

->Signed at Brieg, December 10, Year 1641.<-
->Hans Dieterich von Tschesch, Princely<-
->Privy Councilor there.<-

<summary>This page contains correspondence from Hans Dieterich von Tschesch defending Jacob Böhme's first work, *Aurora*, as a foundational text for understanding Böhme's philosophy. He argues that despite Böhme's later use of more complex technical language, this early work captures his "naked gift" of divine insight most clearly for beginners.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Aurora, Hans Dieterich von Tschesch, Balthasar Walther, Brieg, philosophy, divine illumination, Silesian mysticism</keywords>

<meta>This page serves as a concluding note to the Table of Contents of Jacob Böhme's *Aurora*. It provides a crucial bibliographical detail regarding the provenance of the notes found within the text, linking them directly to Böhme’s later revisions in the manuscript owned by his patron, Michael von Ender.</meta>

## Note.

All <term>annotations</term> <note>original: *Annotationes*; explanatory notes or comments added to a text.</note> that are printed in this book in <term>median type</term> <note>original: *Median-Buchstaben*; a medium-sized font used in early modern printing to distinguish specific commentary from the main body text.</note> and have been enclosed in <term>parentheses</term> <note>original: *Parentesi*; here referring specifically to the square brackets "[ ]" shown in the text.</note> in this manner "[ ]" were added by the author himself several years later, and were written with his own hands into the manuscript of Michael von Ender.

<summary>This note explains that specific bracketed annotations in the text are the author's own later additions, transcribed from the manuscript belonging to Michael von Ender.</summary>
<keywords>annotations, Jacob Böhme, Michael von Ender, manuscript history, typography, editorial notes</keywords>
